## 2026-10-21 ✅ The simulated player and the verified boards: one log, and the same state twice (NEXT 18 g)

A scripted player plays a starter's whole opening, the first outing the content has and the first sale through the real HTTP API of `server/`, and the state the server ends on must equal the same log replayed from `newState` on the laptop: `serialize`, byte for byte. All three starters run. The phone is the real client - `RemoteSession` over `fetchWire`, the code the game itself runs - not a test-local post; the store is postgres 16 in CI and in memory without it, and the identity mode is the real `AUTH_MODE=dev-header` gate.
- **The clock.** The run spans sixteen game hours (the opening ends at the night arch, and the ruins' night is the doorstep pit's toll and the wolf's round, so the outing is walked by day), which a real phone spends sitting still. The script moves the API's own `ApiOptions.now` seam with the game time it claims and never past it, so the time proof is not bypassed: a step claiming more than the clock paid for is still refused, as `server/test/api.test.ts` shows on its own.
- **The books are the run.** The `action_log` and `gold_ledger` rows the request wrote are compared with the log the player sent, entry for entry, and the client's own mirror (rebuilt from the reply patches) with the server's state.
- **The first expedition.** `bundle.expeditions` is empty: the night ruins of NEXT 14b are not built. The outing is therefore the first one the content supports - past the night arch, the gate stones of the ruins dug by day and the deep ruins once the gate has taught the trowel's way, then the tangle's heart and home to the elder (NEXT 6). The script keeps the outing as its own step and carries a TODO for 14b, so the real expedition joins it as content and not as code.
- **The boards.** `server/src/boards.ts` scores nothing it has not proved: 18 f's `checkAccount` replays the account's own log from its start state under the seed column to the stored row byte for byte, asks the gold ledger for exactly the grants that log makes and for both sums to be the purse, and a log whose versions skip is refused before anything is read from it. A tampered row, a hole and a grant with no action behind it are each refused before the transaction opens, and the board it would have joined is left as it was. The measures are CR-40's, read off the verified state: worth (gold plus the bag at the trader's pay), collection (the journal), levels (every profession added up). Places count nothing while houses and plots are paused. The proof is 18 f's, not a second copy of it.
- **CI.** One step, `pnpm exec vitest run server/test/sim.test.ts server/test/boards.test.ts` against the same postgres service container: 3.9 s on the laptop (three starters at 0.55-0.70 s, the boards at 1.0-1.3 s), well inside the three minutes 18 g allows.

**Retry when:** NEXT 14b lands (the outing becomes the expedition 18 g names), or an account's log outgrows a whole replay per board post (then a signed checkpoint per week, decided before it is built - a board that trusts a stored number is the thing this entry exists to refuse). ## 2026-10-21 ✅ The books on the server: one `gold_ledger` row per grant, and an account's log that plays again (NEXT 18 f)

The gold book and the action log now have one definition each, in `server/src/ledger.ts`, and the server and the proof read the same one.
- **What a request books** (`bookedGoldMg`): one row per `gold` event the step reports, in order, a zero never booked - so a sale is one row and a purchase is one row even in one request. The opening is the one grant the kernel pays without an event (`start` sets the purse from `params.starting_gold_mg` and adds it to the state's own ledger), so the server books it here from the same number, or a purse paid by content would stand in the state with nothing behind it in the book. Today `starting_gold_mg` is 0 and the row is never written; a test raises it on a copied bundle to prove the row appears.
- **The draws stay the seed's.** `draw(seed, entity, counter)` in `kernel/src/rng.ts` is unchanged: the seed is the server's secret (R4), the counters live in the state, so the luck of a replay is the luck of the run - nothing else. A careless gather pays nothing by itself and adds one unit when `drawBasisPoints(seed, node, draws)` stands under `missKeepBp`; a test reads that rule off the stored state instead of restating it.
- **The replay** (`replayAccount`): `newState(bundle, seed)`, then every `action_log` row at its own tick with the server's rules (`validateTravel`), one step per movement report inside a `moves` batch. The first refusal is a finding, never a repair.
- **The check** (`checkAccount`) asks three questions of the books and returns the problems it finds: the replayed text is the stored text (`serialize`, with the first byte they part at named); the ledger holds exactly the rows the log implies, as a multiset, so a forged row is "a grant with no action behind it" and a missing one is named too; and the ledger's sum is the state's purse, down to the milli-gold, with the state's own `minted - burned` agreeing.
- **Where it is proven:** `server/test/luck.test.ts` plays each of the three starts' opening through the real API over HTTP - start, own task, keystone, the bridge's 30000, a felled oak, one berry bought, one log sold, one careless strike - and checks the books of every run; the forged row, the removed row, the removed action, one milli-gold of tampering and a seed on the other side of the draw all fail. `server/test/db.test.ts` plays the forager's run through postgres and checks the rows as postgres returns them (bigint as text, jsonb as written). The moving parts of a phone and the script live in `server/test/support/phone.ts`, which NEXT 18 g reuses.
**Retry when:** NEXT 18 g posts a score to a board (it reads this log and this check), or a second drawing site makes a row-per-event too thin (then a `seq` column on `gold_ledger` ties each row to the log row that made it, a migration of its own).
**Model:** GitHub Copilot (Copilot SDK, model not named to the session; manager: game-94).

## 2026-10-21 ✅ Sign-in as built: Google with PKCE, an email link behind a button, a revocable cookie, and an age gate that errs toward refusing (NEXT 18 e)

`server/src/auth/` and migration `0003_signin.sql`; the API starts in it with `AUTH_MODE=session`. Choices the spec left open, as built:
- **Google** is the code flow with PKCE (S256), a `state` bound to a `__Host-` cookie and held hashed in `oauth_flows` for one answer, and a nonce. The id token from the token endpoint is checked in full (RS256 against Google's published keys, `iss`, `aud`, `exp`, the nonce, `email_verified === true`) and its claims are used; no userinfo call, so the one answer trusted is the signed one. The app is project `openfold-game`, in Testing, with one test user; the client JSON is in the owner's secrets folder.
- **The email link** is a single-use token kept as its sha-256, 15 minutes, three per address in that time, and the request answers the same for every address. Opening the link changes nothing: the page posts the token only when the player presses its button, so a mail scanner that follows links cannot spend it. The only mailer writes the link to the log; the sender waits for h5.
- **A proven identity** signs in to the account it names, or joins the account its proven address already has, or waits on a sign-up ticket (30 minutes). The sign-up takes a name (the kernel's rule, unique in the world in any case), a year of birth and the player's yes to one account per person (CR-53); the country, sent by the phone, only sets the age and is not kept.
- **The age** (CR-49): only the year is asked, so the gate counts the age surely reached (one less than the difference) and errs toward refusing; 13, or the higher age of digital consent of the countries listed in `age.ts` (GDPR article 8 and Korea), and the highest of them (16) when no country is sent. Under-age ends the ticket, so a second year needs a new proof. The list is the commonly published one, not legal advice: the owner confirms it before the first address is collected.
- **The session** is a random token in a `__Host-of_session` cookie (httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, 30 days) and a `sessions` row by its hash, rotated at every sign-in and revoked at sign-out; a POST stands for no one unless its Origin is the API's or the game's.
- **Closing an account** found that the append-only books of 18 b refused the cascade, so no account could be closed; `0003` lets a removal through only once its account is gone, and a direct `delete` on a book still raises.
- **Discord** is a slot that answers "not open yet": its OAuth app needs the owner's own login.
Nothing goes public before the Fable auth review the owner asked for.
**Retry when:** the review asks for a change; the owner confirms the age list or asks for a month of birth; a real mailer is chosen (h5); Discord's app exists. Model: claude-opus-5-5 (game-65).
## 2026-10-21 ✅ The books key a grant by the step that paid it, on both sides (the follow-up game-94 named on #268)

#268 booked a `gold_ledger` row with the whole request - a `moves` batch, at the state's tick after it - while `replayAccount` booked the same grant with the single `move` and the report's own tick. Nothing pays gold inside a batch today, so no row was wrong yet; the day a step inside one pays, `checkAccount` would have called an honest row forged.
- **One rule, called twice.** `goldRowsOf(bundle, steps)` in `server/src/ledger.ts` turns a list of played steps - action, tick, events - into the gold rows of a request. `server/src/api.ts` plays its request into that list (one step for `/action`, one step per report for `/moves`) and books through it; `replayAccount` books each of its steps through it. The two cannot key a grant differently, because there is one place that does the keying.
- **`Books` carries the rows, not the deltas.** `Books.gold` is `LedgerRow[]` (`deltaMg`, `action`, `tick`) instead of `goldDeltasMg: number[]`, and `store-pg` writes each row's own action and tick. A zero is still never booked.
- **Proof.** `server/test/luck.test.ts`: the three starters' runs now compare the server's rows with the replay's rows *element for element* ("the server's gold rows and the replay's gold rows are the same rows, action and tick and all"), and a batch that pays on its middle report is booked as that report, at its tick, with the batch's later tick ignored ("a payment inside a movement batch is booked as the report that paid it, at the report's tick"). `server/test/api.test.ts` asserts a real batch's `books.gold` is empty and the log row is still the batch.
- **What is not done here.** No step inside a batch pays gold in today's content, and content cannot make one: a move changes the tile, and every gold event comes from buy, sell, turn-in or the opening's purse. The batch case is proven where the two sides meet (the step list), and the kernel is unchanged; the day a move pays, the row is keyed right with no change to this rule.
**Retry when:** a step inside a batch pays for real (then the batch's gold rows can be asserted end to end through the API), or a board reads `gold_ledger.action` as a request rather than as the step that paid.
**Model:** GitHub Copilot (Copilot SDK, model not named to the session; manager: game-94).
## 2026-10-21 ✅ The age gate aligned with the big companies: a full date of birth, no retry for a day, and the country from our proxy (fix/signin-age)

The owner's answer on #272's two age points was "align with the big companies" (Epic, Roblox, Supercell, and the FTC's neutral age screen). What changed against #272:
- **A full date of birth** (`birthDate`, `YYYY-MM-DD`) replaces the year. The gate counts the exact age on the server's UTC date: the birthday counts from its own day, and a 29 February birthday from 1 March in other years. Only `birth_band` is stored, never the date. This also answers #272's second point: a player who turns 13 later this year is no longer refused today.
- **A neutral screen**: the refusal names no age, and an under-age answer sets `__Host-of_age` (httpOnly, 24 h). While it stands, that browser's sign-up page and form both answer `under-age`, even after a new proof of the address. The ticket still ends.
- **The country comes from our proxy, never from the player.** The `country` field is refused. The API reads the header named by `COUNTRY_HEADER` (nginx sets `X-Country` from GeoIP and overwrites a client's value). Unset, missing, or not one capital code, it holds the sign-up to the highest age (16).
- **The list** keeps the EU article 8 values and KR 14, adds CN 14 (PIPL), and is reviewed whenever the game opens to a new country.
- **Where we differ from the big companies**: an under-age player gets the offline demo, not a parent-consent "cabined" account. We hold no child data at all, so there is no parental consent flow to build or to get wrong.
Proof: `server/test/signin.test.ts` (birthday edges at 13 and 16, the refusal cookie, the header trusted only when configured, a missing header) and `server/test/boot.test.ts` (`COUNTRY_HEADER`).
**Retry when:** the game opens to a country with a different age (review the list); the owner wants accounts for younger players (then a parent-consent flow, and the data rules that come with it); GeoIP proves too coarse at a border. Model: claude-opus-5-5 (game-65).

## 2026-10-21 ✅ The sun moves south: every cast shadow falls away from the viewer (GK2 B, #256)

The entry above, "The GK2 method (B)", says what B does to decision C: the sun stands south, the fronts of the standing
things take the light and the backs the shade, and the cast shadow points away from the viewer instead of toward it. That
entry left the change to the pull request that builds the pass. This is it, in #256.

- **The data.** Every key of `content/core/params.json` `light.sun.keys` keeps its `right` and turns its `down`
  negative: -39 at 06:00, -37 at 08:00, -16 at 10:00, -11 at 12:00, -16 at 14:00, -37 at 16:00, -41 at 16:30, -39 at
  18:00. The lengths are the old ones to the third digit: 0.936 of the caster's height at 08:00, 1.099 at 16:30, 0.11 at
  noon, which is 10.6 art px on a 64-px caster. So the morning shadow leans up-left, the evening up-right, and the noon
  shadow straight back.
- **The schema follows.** `SunKey.down` is -100..100: a negative `down` is the sun in the south, a real value now and not
  a typo, and `schema/test/params-sun.test.ts` pins every key below zero.
- **The depth order holds, with no change to the pass.** Both shadow layers sit at 2.5 and below, under the shallowest
  thing `world.ts` lets cast (`DEPTH.puff`, 3.5). A shadow falling north therefore lies behind its caster, the caster is
  drawn over it, and a thing standing nearer covers the shadow of the thing behind it - which is what a sun in the south
  asks for and what the old direction could not have. The one thing the pass loses is the shadow that spills in front of
  the viewer: there is none now, by the decision, not by a bug.
- **What the frames show.** [`2026-10-21-cast-08.png`](docs/design/frames/2026-10-21-cast-08.png) and
  [`-1630.png`](docs/design/frames/2026-10-21-cast-1630.png), phone size at 3x (390x844, 1170x2532), the bridge corner:
  the rocks, the sign post and the player throw their shadows up-left at 08:00 and up-right at 16:30. A shadow pointing
  away is mostly behind its caster, so what the eye reads is the part that spills past the silhouette; on the sand of the
  16:30 frame the ink `#1b1a2e` measures 0.29 to 0.31 across its three channels, and over that sand the same frame with
  the pass switched off holds none of it: 0 pixels against 4,106.
- STYLE section 4's Direction row is rewritten to this, as the GK2 entry asked, in the same pull request.

## 2026-10-21 ✅ Free movement as built: the feet walk the pixels, the world and the kernel stay on tiles (NEXT 22)

The owner, 18.10: "free movement, not a fixed feel"; and 21.10: "free movement now, but graphics come first for me", so this landed after the art and light streams took the machine. What the entry below builds, in the builder's own file, next to the rules it walks by.
- **The walk** (`game/src/walking.ts`, `Walker`): the feet's centre at art-pixel precision, a velocity from the held input at one tile per `walk_step_ms` whatever the angle (two arrows at once, or the stick at any angle, are normalised to one length), and no acceleration yet. The tile under the feet's centre is the player's, and every tile the centre enters is reported to the kernel in order.
- **Collision**: a body box at the feet, 28 x 16 art px (`BODY_BOX`, a tunable constant beside the walk), against the tiles `canEnter` refuses. x and y are resolved apart so the body slides along a wall, and because the box has width it never cuts the corner between two refused tiles. A straight walk that meets a corner is eased onto its lane while the motion is blocked, so a corridor slightly off the body's line does not stop it. A frame longer than a tile takes (a slow machine, a tab coming back) walks one tile at most, so one frame never carries the body through a tile and the tile under the feet stays a place a hand can stop on.
- **Four-connected reports (G56)**: the axes are resolved one after the other inside a frame, so a centre that crosses a corner reports the side tile first, at the same frame. A tile is reported once, and only when the centre enters it.
- **Facing**: the dominant axis of the motion with a hysteresis of 1.25, so a walk near 45 degrees does not flicker. The three drawn directions and the mirror are unchanged; a diagonal walk shows its dominant facing, as Stardew Valley does. The faced tile is the tile beside the feet's tile, and every action that read `pos + DIRS[facing]` reads `facedTile(s)`.
- **The stride**: today's walk frames advanced by the distance walked (one stride per tile), not by a step count.
- **The phone** (`game/src/input.ts`, `game/src/ui/touch.ts`): the pad is a stick. The direction is the finger's offset at any angle, the dead zone stays 12% of the width, and full speed comes at 40%. A and B, and the keys, are unchanged.
- **Both doors**: the 3D door walks by the same `Walker` in the same pull request, with the hero's yaw taken from the motion itself.
- **The server's check (NEXT 22 c)**: the reachability stays four-way, and the charge becomes the path's octile length in one kernel function, `walkCharge` (`STEP_MILLI` 1000, a turning pair `DIAGONAL_PAIR_MILLI` 1414). Charging a diagonal walk by its four-way length drained the pool and refused an honest walk after about 15 s. `KERNEL_VERSION` is 0.14.0. G56's line carries the number.
- **The e2e (NEXT 22 d)**: `game/e2e/walk.ts` holds the input until the feet's centre stands on a tile, with a timeout, in place of counted presses; the opening, stealth and 3D runs all walk through it, and the opening's phone player pushes the pad's stick. The page lets go on the frame the tile is reached: a release sent over the wire lands a frame late, and on a machine rendering in software a frame is a quarter of a second. A walk stops where it stands when a window opens under it (the word in town stops the walk) and waits out a world held by a window that closes.
Proof: `game/test/walk.test.ts` (the four measurable lines of NEXT 22 without a renderer: 10 tiles in 1.9 s straight and 7.07 on each axis diagonally, stopping in the frame of release, 200 frames of walls at 64 angles with no overlap and four-connected reports, the faced tile after a diagonal walk for pick, dig and talk), `kernel/test/travel.test.ts` (the octile charge), `server/test/api.test.ts` (a 20 s diagonal walk passes; a 3-tile jump is still refused); the opening, stealth and tending e2e in CI.
**Retry when:** the owner judges the feel on a preview and wants different speed, acceleration or stick numbers (polish); a phone player misses the tile meant for an action (the faced tile is the thing to widen); a predator's watch reads oddly with the body between two tiles. Model: claude-opus-5-5 (game-b1), finished by deepseek-walk.
## 2026-10-21 ✅ A step lifts its leg in the frame it starts: the walk check's flake was the client's, at a slow frame rate

The opening e2e failed on CI with "stepping left/right/down never lifted a leg: frames 1 in 2 drawn" (develop run 39598103878 attempt 1, #252, #256): every retry drew a step in exactly two frames, both feet together. The cause is in `tryStep` (`game/src/scenes/world.ts`): a step began on the standing frame, and the next update added a whole frame's delta, so at frames over 133 ms (`STRIDE` 0.7 of `STEP_MS` 190) it was already past the stride and showed the landing. The test was right; a slow phone walked without legs. The fix sets the stride frame (0 or 2) in the update the step starts, so its first drawn frame lifts a leg at any frame rate; the frames after it are unchanged.
Measured by a probe of 30 single steps from rest on the desktop door (`.scratch`, not committed): CPU throttle 8, before 15 of 30 steps showed no leg, after 0 of 30; throttle 4, 1 of 30 against 0; unthrottled, 0 against 0. No sleep and no timeout changed; the e2e is untouched. Five local runs of the opening e2e on a loaded laptop: the walk check passed in all ten doors (every step showed a leg frame); four runs passed whole, and run 4 failed on another check, "a step away still cut logs" (phone): the first step-away press came 7 s after the A that started the felling, slowed by the swing screenshot, and the felling had ended by then. That is a timing gap of its own in the test, left for a separate fix.
**Retry when:** a walk sheet gets more than three frames, or the stride's timing changes (then the first frame of a step is chosen by the new table). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-21 ✅ The opening handover as built: the phone's log is replayed, kept under a secret seed, or the account starts fresh (NEXT 18 d)
The phone keeps every step since the start (cap `OPENING_LOG_MAX` 2000, saved with the game, rolled back with cancelled work) and uploads `{seed, log, state}` with the sign-up. The server plays it again from `newState(seed)` with the kernel's travel check and the `/moves` movement credit, refuses a second start, time running backwards, more than one hour of game time or more than 150 gold of pay (purse plus what the bag sells for), and keeps it only when the replay serializes to the phone's state. The state then takes a secret seed. Anything else: a not-started state under the secret seed, and the box says so in one line.
Books: a kept opening is the account's first `action_log` row `{kind:'opening', seed, log}` with its gold rows, so `checkAccount` proves a handed-over account (test: handover.test.ts). The account, its state and its books are one transaction (signin-db test).
Open for the owner: the two caps are mine (an honest opening carries about 80 gold of quests); the client wire is same-origin and the server has no CORS, so a build with `VITE_GAME_SERVER` on another origin needs the h6 cutover first; a phone that walked faster than the movement credit allows loses its opening, not its account.
Model: opus. Link: PR feat/handover-opening.

## 2026-10-21 ✅ The opening handover's two caps stand: one hour of game time, 150 gold of worth
The builder of 18 d (#277) set the caps an uploaded opening is held to: at most one hour of game time (`OPENING_TIME_MAX_MS`) and at most 150 gold of worth, the purse plus what the bag sells for (`OPENING_PAY_MAX_MG`, in `server/src/handover.ts`). The opening runs about ten minutes and its quests pay 80 gold, so an honest opening fits with room; a forged one can carry no more than the caps, and an opening past either starts the account fresh.
The owner, 21.10: approved as built.
**Retry when:** the opening's pay or length changes (the test "the caps leave an honest opening room" fails first), or honest players are seen starting fresh. Model: claude-opus-5-5 (the manager, game-1b); the numbers are game-65's.

## 2026-10-21 ✅ The palette deepens: the 32 stay, the world's ramps are shared (the owner, on the rich-style evidence)

The rich-style experiment (`docs/art/RICH_STYLE_EXPERIMENT.md`, PR exp/rich-style) put the same five objects through the same model and importer with only the palette and the style line changed, and the imported sprite's colour richness went from about a quarter of the reference's to half to four fifths. On that, the owner, 21.10: **deepen**.
The shape is the one fixed on 20.10 (ART_SYSTEM section 3): the 32 core colours do not move, the depth is ramps of five or six hue-shifted steps, and a sprite that names none is valid as it is. What this entry adds:
- **The ramps of the world are shared.** `CORE_RAMPS` in `tools/src/kit/palette.ts`: 13 ramps of six steps (grey, stone, green, moss, rust, wood, bark, water, teal, red, plum, fire, skin), 78 colours with the core, each holding to the ramp rule in `ramps.ts` (`kit-ramps.test.ts`), each core family carried inside its own ramp so a sprite drawn in the core wood sits inside `wood`. A shared ramp needs no `ramps` block in a kit file; a sprite names the ones it draws in (`ramps` in its kit entry, one to four, the first gives the outline). A kit's own `ramps` stay for materials the shared set lacks, and a kit that repeats a shared id with other steps is refused by `pnpm check`. So no protected path changes, and the schema's four-ramp cap on a kit holds.
- **`kit:gen` draws in them.** An entry's `ramps` names shared ramps; the importer snaps onto the 32 plus their steps and the manifest line carries the names for the kit entry. `"style": "rich"` asks for painterly pixel art and dithers between neighbouring steps; with no `ramps` of its own it draws in every shared ramp. An outside reference that names ramps sets its palette decision to `ramp-added` (PROP_FROM_REF step 0b, rewritten).
- **A new material is still the owner's.** Sand, brass, anything no shared ramp holds: a new ramp in `palette.ts`, by owner decision, in its own pull request, before the sprite that needs it.
- **Nothing of today's content changed.** Every kit of today names no ramp and passes unchanged (the check test). The depth arrives sprite by sprite through `kit:gen`: the 64 px waves from their first sprite, the existing props and items redrawn from themselves (a kit sprite id as `ref`, `ramps` per material), the cast after L1.
**Retry when:** a shared ramp reads wrong beside the core in a still (then its steps move, in `palette.ts`, with the test); a phone window passes 96 colours once sprites name ramps (then the window count becomes an error, as ART_SYSTEM proposed); or the schema's description of a sprite's `ramps` ("of this kit") misleads a creator (then the wording changes with `pnpm schemas`).
Model: claude-fable-5-1; the numbers are the experiment's.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ No way-finding rule anywhere in the game; G69 stays

**No way-finding rule.** This is an open world: whoever sets out for another world explores on their own. Landmarks stand where the story or the look puts them, never by a spacing count. **G69 stays:** paths wind, no straight run over 8 tiles (12 on a bridge); it is a look rule, not a way-finding rule. `pnpm check` now measures it on every map that carries a plan: each planned path's runs and every straight row or column of path letters on the ground.
The owner, 23.10.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The first night is held by size, not by a rule (G71)

**The first night is unavoidable by size.** No rule forbids leaving the first town; the ground is simply too large to cross before dusk, so the first night's quest is a must by geometry. No gate, flag or quest lock enforces it; the region gates of decision 25 stay what they are, physical obstacles a quest clears.
As built: `tools/src/mapplan.ts` (`firstNight`) walks four ways from every wake tile of the pool, the valley's own mends made (the bridge, the arch) and every gate to another world shut, to the nearest tile of another world (a map region with its own `world`) or the map's edge. The walk at `walk_step_ms` a step must take longer than the daylight left at waking (tick 0, noon, to the first dusk at 20:00: 400 s with today's `day_length_ms`), or `pnpm check` fails the map. It also measures the walk to the nearest shut gate, which is where a region quest would open the way. Tests: `tools/test/mapplan.test.ts`.
The owner, 23.10.
**Retry when:** a region quest can be finished before the first dusk (then the walk to that gate is what holds the night, and the check must count the gate open), or the clock or the walk speed changes. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ G71 measures the road beyond each exit, every gate open

**The gate never holds the first night; the road beyond it does.** The gates stay where the story wants them, but beyond every exit of the first region a road runs long enough that, with every gate open, no wake reaches the next region's ground before the first dusk. This replaces the shut-gate measure of the entry above.
As built: `plan.exits[]` (additive): `id`, the gate `slot` or the `at` tile of a way with no slot (a jetty), `to` (a plan region) and `min_road_steps`. `firstNight` walks with every listed gate open; reaching another world or the edge by any way that is not a listed exit before dusk fails the map. `exitRoads` names each exit's least road: the daylight in steps less the fastest wake's steps to the gate, plus `ROAD_MARGIN` (10); a smaller `min_road_steps` fails, and so does a stitched map whose next world's ground starts closer beyond the gate. Tests: `tools/test/mapplan.test.ts`.
The owner, 23.10.
**Retry when:** the clock or the walk speed changes (the numbers move), or a way to travel faster than walking lands. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The rich wave lands: 35 picks by eye in the kit, pebbles and the small rock wait

The owner picked 35 of the wave's 37 entries by eye, over five rounds of the two specs of the wave (`tools/kit-gen/rich-items.json`, `tools/kit-gen/rich-props-small.json`). What the five rounds showed:

- **SDXL won every free pick it was offered.** With rewritten prompts SDXL and its pixel-art LoRA ran beside Stable Diffusion 1.5 on the same entries; every pick from those rounds went to SDXL, 15 of the 35.
- **What the free model misreads goes to Codex.** Ten entries the free models kept drawing as another object (the branch drawn as food) went to Codex with the kit sprite as the reference and two picks of this wave as style examples; eight of them were picked.
- **pebbles and rock_small wait.** The Codex credits ran out during that round, so the two keep today's 16 px sprites until a draw passes the eye.
The owner, 23.10: picked the 35 in the gallery by eye; pebbles and rock_small keep their sprites for now.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The whole game is rich: every sprite at 64 px per tile in the rich style, held by the check

The rich wave (the two entries of 23.10 above) set the look by eye. From here it is the look of the whole game: every sprite of every kit - the ground, the props, the items and the cast - is drawn at 64 art pixels per tile (`density` 4), in kit:gen's rich style (painterly pixel art: hue shifts inside a surface, ambient light, texture, dithered between the steps of the shared ramps), in the 32 core colours and the shared ramps it names, and picked by eye, best of three. A 16 px sprite stays only until its wave redraws it.
- **The code holds it, not this line.** `pnpm check` refuses a sprite at `density` 1 unless the core kit still waits to redraw it. Those sprites are listed by name in `tools/src/kit/rich.ts` (145 today: the cast's 74, 37 ground tiles, 34 props); a listed name drawn at 64 must leave the list, a name the kit no longer holds is refused, and a rich sprite of the core kit names its ramps. The list only shrinks: a wave that lands takes its names off and nothing is added (`tools/test/kit-rich.test.ts`).
- **kit:gen draws rich by default.** An entry with no `style` is rich, and `"style": "kit"`, the flat two-tone look of the 16 px kit, is refused with a pointer to this entry. A sprite with no `ramps` draws in every shared ramp; a tile draws only in the ramps it names (STYLE section 9), and its prompt asks for no outline. A test runs every spec in `tools/kit-gen/` through the same validation, so a spec that breaks the rule fails in CI, not only when someone draws with it.
- **What stays outside.** The UI frame keeps the rules of its own entry (2026-10-20, Field Kit: the 32 colours, no new colours, no gradients, no radius). The 3D door is not pixel art: the rule is truly 2D and stays in the classic game (AGENTS.md, one game, two doors). The word also names the valley's enrichment behind `?rich=` (2026-10-09) and the richness warning of a phone window (2026-10-19); neither is this rule.
- **The order inside it.** The cast's look is a direction: its sprites stay on the list until the ladder of stills picks it (2026-10-20, the art system), while the waves that wait on no pick - the ground, and the props and items redrawn from themselves (2026-10-21) - go ahead. pebbles and rock_small wait for a draw that passes the eye (the entry above).
Supersedes, in part, the 2026-10-21 entry on the palette: `"style": "rich"` is no longer something an entry asks for; it is the only style.
The owner, 23.10: the whole game is to be rich, as the wave drew it, and the code, not only this log, is to hold it.
**Retry when:** a 64 px sprite reads too busy at phone size beside its neighbours in the live game, the ladder of stills picks a look for the cast that the rich style cannot draw, or a 2K phone cannot hold its frame budget with the 64 px kit. Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-23 ✅ Weight and the three paces: places say how many, weight says how fast

**Every item names a whole weight** (0 to 20; most things 1, a log, a stone or the keystone 3, a herb, a sun leaf or a coin 0), and the schema refuses an item without one. **The weight limit is 30 and 10 for each point of might** (`load.base`, `per_point.might`), read as the grade leaves might. **Three walking paces:** light up to three fifths of the limit at 170 ms a tile, steady up to the limit at 190 ms (the old `walk_step_ms`, so every walk the world was measured at is the steady one), heavy above it at 230 ms. The server credits a walk at the pace the load allows, so a heavy player cannot report a light walk. **Nothing more is picked up past one and a half times the limit** ("Too heavy to carry more. Lighten the load first."). **A heavy load in a fight brings breath back at half speed** (`load.heavy_breath_permille` 500); no fight reads it yet, so the rule is fixed in the kernel (`breathRefillPermille`) for the combat wave. Places and weight are two numbers with two jobs: might no longer adds places, it raises the limit. **Things of one kind share a place, up to ten** (`place_stack` 10): a place holds a stack, as in the owner's Stardew reference for the 12, and the weight still counts every unit, so a bag of stones slows you long before it runs out of places. What is on the body takes no place but still weighs: a worn thing, and one of each tool, which hangs at the belt (a spare joins the stacks).
The owner, 23.10 (asked one by one): every item has a weight; light-load limit 30 + 10 per might point; three paces, light, steady, heavy, at 170 / 190 / 230 ms a tile as proposed; a heavy load in a fight refills breath at half speed.
Defaults taken from the recommendation, not asked: the bands at 60 and 100 percent of the limit and the hard edge at 150 percent; worn clothes and tools leave the places but weigh (one of each tool, so a spare axe still takes a place); the item weights above. Not on the page, chosen here: a place holds a stack of ten of one kind. With one unit a place, 12 places fill in the opening's afternoon (the collapsed hall's shards) and a dig there is refused before Pell's bowl turns up; a stack keeps the 12 and the opening as played.
**Retry when:** a playtest finds the light pace never reached after the opening, or the heavy pace reached in the opening itself; or the combat wave reads breath and half speed proves too soft or too hard. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

**The back is one gear slot, holding a bag or a quiver** (`back` joins the worn slots in the schema and the kernel); an archer pays in bag space. **Places come from what is on the back:** nothing there gives 12 (`bag_base`), a satchel 24, a frame pack 36, and the frame pack also adds 10 to the weight limit (`wear.places`, `wear.load_bonus`). A bag comes off only while the rest still fits. **The first bag, the satchel, is made at the valley workshop** (two reed cords and a woven mat, workshop level 3) **and Pell sells it too.** The frame pack is in the content with no source yet: it is the city's.
The owner, 23.10: no bag 12 places, satchel 24, frame pack 36 (frame pack also +10 light-load limit); the back holds a bag or a quiver; the satchel is made at the valley workshop and Pell also sells it.
Defaults taken, not asked: the satchel's recipe numbers, and both bags drawn with the basket sprite until their own art lands.
**Retry when:** the opening's first outing has no room for its finds without a sale at Pell's first (the simulated player sells at Pell's before the ruins whenever it has fewer than four places free), the stack of ten makes the 12 places feel endless in a playtest, or the satchel comes too late. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ One body fact per race, and the skeleton's profile

**The skeleton is stamina 4, might 1** (senses 1, luck 1, agility 0, spirit 0; seven points as every race). **Each race has one body fact, equal in size** (`body` on the race, effects from a closed list, each once): the skeleton is *mended, not fed* - a rest by a fire mends double, food fills half (a bandage or a first-aid kit is not food); the elf has a *light frame* - its load counts four fifths for pace; the human *packs well* - two more places in any bag, never in bare hands. The title's race card shows the body fact.
The owner, 23.10: skeleton stamina 4, might 1, total unchanged, because every trait must also pay off; one body fact per race as above. The human's +4 was sized for bags of 20/40/60; on bags of 12/24/36 it keeps its share as +2 (stated to the owner, follows the recommendation).
Built so far: the food half, the elf's pace and the human's places play now. The double mend waits for a rest to exist (G32): the kernel holds it (`restMendPermille`, 2000 for the skeleton) for the first rest or sleep by a fire to read.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The owner's answers on the bag: stacks, the belt, putting things down

Answers to the open questions of the races-and-bags entries above, given the same day.

**A place holds a stack of ten of one kind** (`place_stack` 10), and **Pell's satchel stays at 290 gold**, the floor its value sets: both kept as built. **Tools:** the tool in the hand takes no place - the one worn there, or else one of the bag's tools, since a hand is never empty of one - and **one more tool hangs at the belt** with no place: one, not one of each kind (the kernel lets them take the tools whose last stack holds just one, so the count never depends on order); every other tool takes a place. **A full bag says so with a small alert by the bag button that blinks over its first few seconds**, besides the one line. **Putting things down:** a "Put down" button in the bag lays one on the tile the player stands on; it stays there, in the save (`player.ground`), until a "Pick up" on that tile takes it back, as much as the places and the weight allow. Both steps are kernel actions (`drop`, `pickup`), pure and replayed by the server like any other. The 2D door draws the pile; the 3D door offers the pick-up without drawing it yet.

For the combat wave, recorded now and not built: in a fight the tools swap by themselves; a full bag in a fight gives the same short blinking alert; an axe fights, but weakly. The satchel and the frame pack keep the basket picture: their drawings were made but are not in the kit yet.

The owner, 23.10: keep the stack of ten and the 290 gold; the hand tool takes no place and only ONE base tool hangs at the belt, every other tool takes a place; picking up with a full bag shows a small alert that repeats for the first few seconds; combat swaps tools automatically, a full bag gives the same alert, an axe fights but weakly; add a drop that leaves the thing on the tile to pick up again, deterministic, saved, with a button in the bag; the double mend goes to whichever of the inn and this lands second; use the new satchel and frame pack art if it is in the kit.
**Retry when:** a playtest loses track of what lies on the ground, or the belt rule surprises a player who carries two tools. Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-23 ✅ The skill tree's format: schools, nodes and the build, added beside the techniques

A new core-only content kind, `school` (`content/core/schools/*.json`), one per profession: a list of nodes, each with a size (small, major or corner), a name and one sentence, its path (`needs`, the same school only), an optional key of attributes it asks for and never spends, and one effect from the closed list. A small node is always on; a major or corner node marked `slot` is a technique and counts only while it sits in the build. A corner node belongs to one race (the trunk of the tree is the race, 22.10 "The tree"). The costs (1, 2, 3 points) and the levels that open the build's five slots (1, 1, 5, 10, 15) are params (`growth`). The kernel learns a node (`learn_node`) and changes the build (`equip`, `unequip`) only at a fire, a camp or in town, and says why it refuses. `Player.nodes` and `Player.build` are optional, so a save from before the tree loads unchanged; FORMAT_VERSION stays 1.
Why: GROWTH.md section 4 asks for the tree to come from content, and the screen needs a format to read before it can be drawn.
Cost: the profession `techniques` stay and still count; the two ways of buying overlap until a later step moves the techniques into the nodes. There is no way to unlearn a node yet. The seed nodes are placeholders drawn from the effects the code reads today (no `see_ripe`, which nothing reads); the content wave writes the real ones.
**Retry when:** the content wave writes the real nodes, or a playtest shows the slots open too early or too late. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The world: a hand-made first map, and a generator that sets the difficulty

The 17.10 seed rings and decision 5's hand-made first map (2252) are combined. The first map is planned and drawn by hand, as decision 5 says. **The generator's job becomes difficulty.** From a place's distance to the game's start and the level the character is expected to have there (from the quests before it and the grinding open to it), it recommends the place's difficulty: how many quests and of what kind, which monsters, how many and how strong, and what else opens there. The same reckoning later grows the world past the first map's edges. NEXT.md section 19 is rewritten to match before any of it is built.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ Search: press A on things to look inside

**Many things in the world can be searched.** Pressing A on a drawer, a cupboard, a book, a shelf or even a stone opens a small window showing what is inside. Sometimes it holds something, often nothing. Out in the open, things can hide easter eggs and rare, valuable gifts; the higher the player's luck, the better the odds of finding them. **Every building gets a random number of furniture pieces**, so it looks lived in, and sometimes an extra one: a statue, or a picture that opens to show its painting. Searchable things are deepened step by step as the game grows. A player cannot put gear into them; the one place that stores gear is Maru's house. Later, in other towns, a guest house lets a player stay in multiplayer.
The owner, 23.10: an idea for the game, like searching drawers in Fallout, as written.
**Retry when:** searching slows the opening's path, or the luck odds make rare gifts common. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The importer keeps the drawing inside its outline, and 14 rich picks are imported again

On develop, 14 of the 35 rich sprites at density 4 had see-through pixels inside their own drawing, 1,845 in all: the stone idol 589 (its face 160 and its chest 146), the trowel 332, the unlit campfire 228, the small mushrooms 169, the hazard trap 153, the herb stew 111, the shelf 91, the basket 67, the herb tea 49, the first-aid kit 30, the axe 16, the herb 5, the fallen branches 4 and the mushroom patch 1. The ground showed through the idol's face and the trowel's blade. The importer's mask (`initialMask` in `tools/src/kit/import.ts`) took every pixel within the threshold of the border's colour (65 to 100) for background, and every magenta-fringe pixel, wherever it stood.
**Background is what lies near the border's colour and reaches the picture's edge through such pixels**, flooded from the edge, and the magenta-fringe rule holds only there. A region of that colour the drawing encloses stays drawing (a pale face, a pink bowl, a plum jar), unless 90% of it lies within a tight distance of the background, the border's own noise plus 8: then it is an opening the drawing leaves, painted in the background colour, and background too. The noise is the 99.5th percentile of the border's distances counted within the threshold, so a drawing that touches the edge is not noise; 90% and not all, because an opening's rim is a pixel or two blended toward the drawing. On the 14 raw drawings the threshold was 65 and the tight distance 12.6 to 35.6; three regions came out as openings (the herb tea's handle, 94% within it, and the two gaps between the small mushrooms' stems, 97%), and the most a region kept as drawing had within it was 89%, a glimpse of the raw's grey ground between the small mushrooms' grass blades, one art pixel, that comes back light grey. Nothing else of the importer changed.
**The check.** `pnpm check` reports at error level a sprite at density 4 whose frame encloses a see-through region of at most 24 px (`tools/src/kit/holes.ts`). A region is its pixels side by side or corner to corner, enclosed when none of it lies on the frame's edge, so a pocket open to the outside at a corner is a notch and passes, as does an opening larger than 24 px. The message names the re-import, `pnpm kit:gen <spec> --only <entry> --reimport --out <run>`, with the raw drawing the sprite's `source` line points at, and the copy over the kit file. On the kit before the re-import it flags 12 of the 35: the basket's one speck joins its opening at a corner, and the herb's pocket opens at one. Density 1 is left out, as 14 of its 145 sprites would fail.
**The re-import.** The 14 are the owner's picks, the same drawings, imported again from their raw drawings with the wave's parameters (the spec entries are those of the runs, and `kit:gen --reimport` gives the same bytes). Enclosed see-through pixels went from 1,845 to 207, and what is left is background in the raw drawings: the herb tea's handle 36, the small mushrooms' gaps 139, and three pockets open at a corner (the herb stew's rim 25, the herb 5, the idol 2). No solid pixel turned see-through except where a sprite moved. The axe has its blade's pale edge back and is one art pixel wider, so all of it is drawn anew: of its 903 solid pixels 423 change colour and 73 turn clear as it sits a pixel further left. The herb stew's bowl is whole, so the importer reads the drawing on its own 8 px art pixels instead of 7, and the stew comes back 42x37 instead of 48x42: 1,017 of its 1,546 solid pixels change, 379 of them to clear, as the smaller stew covers less of its tile. On the other twelve a solid pixel changes colour (0 to 75 per sprite) only where an art pixel it is drawn from now holds pixels the old mask cut. The basket's opening is painted in purple shades in its raw drawing and comes back solid purple, the hazard trap's eyes and nostrils, glowing magenta in its raw drawing, come back in the drawing's purple, and a few one-pixel glimpses of a raw's own ground between grass blades (the small mushrooms, the mushroom patch, the fallen branches) come back grey where the grass showed through.
The before/after on grass goes to the owner before the merge.
**Retry when:** a drawing paints an opening in a shade of the background beyond the border's noise (it then comes back solid: fix the drawing, or widen the margin), or the check reports a speck a drawing means (then the 24 px bound or the corner rule is the question). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The line is PEGI 16 and ESRB T: brutal in taste, never for show (the owner, 19.10; carried from #223)

Carried from #223 (TONE.md), which closes unmerged; the page stays on branch `docs/tone-register` and lands with the first death or combat strings. This entry keeps the owner's word where the next builders read it.
The question (19.10): how mature the dialogue, the violence and the content should read. Three lines were put to him with the games and the ratings on each: a hard world told softly (Minecraft, Stardew Valley, RuneScape: PEGI 7 to 12), brutal in taste (Dark Souls, Darkest Dungeon, Final Fantasy XIV: PEGI 16), brutal on the screen (The Witcher 3, Red Dead Redemption 2, Blasphemous: PEGI 18). His word: "I want it as mature as it can be (not necessarily sexual) but brutal in taste", the second line, "PEGI 16", with the account at 13 as it is.
- **The line is PEGI 16 and ESRB T, and the tighter one holds.** The Dark Souls taste with blood kept small.
- **The words (G22).** Plain words stay (short sentences, concrete nouns, a second-language reader follows every line); the reader of eight is no longer the measure. Death is named death, blood is named when it is in the scene, a character may speak hard by its voice sheet; slurs and sexual words are never said.
- **The images (G46).** A hit may bleed, a person's too; a wound stays afterwards as a bandage, a torn sleeve or a scar; a predator is drawn biting; a body lies a moment. Nothing close up, nothing dwelt on, no gore for its own sake, no suffering for show, no dismemberment, no torture played out, no violence against someone who cannot act, nothing sexual.
- **The death (G33).** The card says "You died", and the body lies where it fell; the kernel's fall keeps its name in the code.
- **The account stays at 13 or the country's higher age (9.10).**
- **The day stays 20 minutes.** Asked whether `day_length_ms` (1,200,000) is too short against other games, he kept the recommendation "for now".
No art changes with this entry: a predator biting, a body in the grass and blood on a sleeve are a direction, shown to the owner as phone-size stills before a sprite is touched (AGENTS.md).
**Supersedes:** "Menace with a limit" (10.10) and its PEGI 12 / ESRB E10+ aim; "a child of eight reads every line" in G22; "fell, never died" in G46, G33 and the review checklist's question 11. The rule pages keep the older words until the pull request that brings TONE.md rewrites them.
**Retry when:** a store's rating comes back at 18 or M (then the scene that pushed it is cut, never the line), or a playtest shows the words reading as a promise of combat before combat is built. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ Open ends of the world plan, the owner's answers of 19.10: players may destroy and the valley repairs, a death leaves a pack, characters keep hours, a lean opens doors, the sinks are the world's prices (carried from #223)

Carried from #223, which closes unmerged. Asked on 19.10 one at a time, three options each with a recommendation. The sketches #223 marked as proposed are not his word and are not carried.
1. **Players may destroy, with a repair loop.** His pick, over the recommendation ("the world wounds, the player does not") and over "nothing is destroyed". In the wild only (danger 1 and up), never in a town, on a plot or a path; a thing a player may break is marked so in the content (a grove by fire, a footbridge, a dam), and every breakable thing carries its repair: a shared quest that eats goods and gold, so the repair is a sink and the loop is the content. Supersedes in part "nobody blocks a way" (10.10) and REACTIONS section 10's "a mark that closes a way: only the world makes one", which now read "until the valley repairs it".
2. **A death leaves a pack anyone may take, gone after a day.** His own answer, past the three offered. The outing's finds lie where the body fell as a pack any player may take, first come, the dead player included; a game day later it is gone. "A fall gains nothing" (10.10) holds: the pack holds no more than the outing found. Supersedes in part G33's "nothing left in the world, nothing marks where the fall was". Combat round 2 (below) makes it the one rule in the marked zone too.
3. **Characters keep hours, and the inn sells the night.** The recommendation, in his words "only broader". Each character has waking hours; at dark they go home or to the inn, the stall closes, and a quest is offered and handed in only while its giver is awake. The night has faces of its own with quests and rumours. The inn sells beer and the night's road food, each with an advantage and a cost ("it has its advantages and its disadvantages"). Read with decisions 27, 32, 36 and 50 of 22.10, which name Maru's hours, the innkeeper, the three night faces and the inn's house.
4. **A lean opens doors, never closes them.** The recommendation. Past a threshold a belief's own people, places and quests open to the player who leaned its way, with a worn mark and a title; the other beliefs' people still talk and trade, and only their own quests wait until the lean is walked back. No war between beliefs, no ending. Decision 34 of 22.10 (beliefs are not bound to races) stands beside it.
5. **How gold leaves the world: the world's prices.** The recommendation. No upkeep (9.10) and no tax on trade; the sinks are what the world sells and does not give back: the stall and the inn, the settlement, the repair of what was destroyed (1), and the pack that vanishes (2).
Not carried: his answer on the skill tree ("something more like The Witcher or Drakantos", the content waits), replaced by decisions 38 and 39 of 22.10. The seventh open end, who the two named persons of moderation are, was left out of that round on his scoping and stays open.
**Retry when:** a playtest shows a ring burned faster than it is repaired, a pack taken by a stranger read as theft rather than the valley's rule, or the inn's night food bought as the only way through the night. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The world's structure, the owner's answers of 19.10: one world, lands of stamped places, a soft edge, a marked zone for fights, travel earned by level, an outside door, a caught bot held (carried from #219)

Carried from #219 (WORLD_STRUCTURE.md section 13 and FOUNDATIONS.md section 11), which closes unmerged; the pages stay on branch `docs/world-structure` and are reread when the open-world stream resumes. Each question was asked with the choices of RuneScape, World of Warcraft and Drakantos beside it.
1. **The unit of content:** an authored place stamped on generated ground, grouped into lands with a name and a story. Rings are the build order only. Read with decisions 2 and 5 of 22.10: the first map is composed by hand and finished before any new region.
2. **The edge:** a soft border. Crossing it, the player vanishes into mist and sees almost nothing, with a countdown toward a fall; when the map grows the mist lifts and what was there stays. Supersedes in part the open world (17.10): a closed grid edge gives way to the soft edge.
3. **Safety far from town:** towns and forts per land with small safe pockets; the promise that a safe place is always within a run may relax far out on purpose, since a large world must not have too many escape hatches; healing is an answer.
4. **One world for everyone:** a split into copies only if a measurement on the live server says one server cannot hold the players, and then the owner decides.
5. **Changing existing areas:** creators ask to change what exists the way they ask to extend the game, by pull request through the automatic checks, with an alert to the core team, which reviews it. Players leave marks and never edit the map.
6. **Fights between players:** a marked zone in the wild where anyone may attack anyone, like Old School RuneScape's Wilderness; everywhere else no player can hurt another.
7. **Travel in a large world:** every faster form is legitimate (an item, a skill, a mount, a teleport to a new or visited place), each earned by player level; none picked, none ruled out. A region's door is still its own quest, regardless of grade or level (decision 25 of 22.10): the level earns a faster way, not a door.
8. **The account:** an external login (Google, Apple, Steam) and no password of the game's own; it adds Apple and Steam to the doors of 18.10.
9. **A caught bot:** the account is held for review and the owner decides; the gains are wiped, never the character.
Not carried, open: his answer on where an outing ends (W7: a fire with a tent from the start, which costs time and resources, takes a place in the bag and keeps everything) reverses the entry of 19.10 "A fire anywhere outside the town is a safe place while it burns; the tent is dropped", which stands on develop. It goes back to him as one question.
**Retry when:** the first ring's measurement on a phone asks for a split (4), or a creator's change to an existing area lands without the core team's review (5). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The quest forge, the owner's four decisions of 19.10: one grammar, quests bound per land, RuneScape in the quests and World of Warcraft in the size, no human per land (carried from #220)

Carried from #220 (QUEST_FORGE.md), which closes unmerged; the plan stays on branch `docs/quest-forge` and comes back with the quest-designer stream's first pull request. 2. Quests are bound per land when the land is made, by a deterministic binder from the world seed and the land's graph, never at run time; the output is committed content checked like any other.

4. No human per land (his addition): the gates decide, the owner reads a digest and retires a storylet when he wants; the one approval left is the library, once per batch.
Read with decisions 10 and 25 of 22.10: a bound quest reacts to the race and never closes to one, and every region opens by its own quest.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ Combat, round 1: real time, like a regular multiplayer game; the server runs every fight on its tick (the owner, 20.10; carried from #243)

Carried from #243 (COMBAT.md, the frame G35 reserved for the owner), which closes unmerged; the page stays on branch `docs/combat-frame` and lands with the combat wave. Its five questions went to him on 20.10, one at a time, three answers each with the recommendation first; rounds 1 to 5 are his answers. The page's kernel shape and rule lines (G78 onward) are proposals and are not carried.
The question: what a fight is made of and on what clock, so a creature fight and a zone fight are one shape. Offered: A, beats of half a second, the phone playing a creature fight and the server replaying its batch (recommended); B, real-time swings replayed from a per-frame log; C, turns. His word (20.10): "real time, like a regular multiplayer game, like League of Legends", his own answer.
Read as (the page's reading): the server runs every fight, against a creature and between players alike, on a fixed tick of its own; the phone sends its inputs over the live socket and draws what the server sends back, predicting only its own walk; a hit lands when the server says it did. What it costs, accepted with the answer: no fight without the live connection, so combat comes after the presence relay; the server's cost per fight is measured before the step ships.
**Supersedes:** the client-played encounter batch of NEXT 18 a and of the 19.10 entry "G35 is the demo's rule" (proposed there, never his word); that entry's "it comes before live presence" now reads "after the presence relay".
**Retry when:** a mobile line's round trip makes a hit feel late in the first ring's fights (then a designed prediction rule with the owner, never a simulation on the phone), or the server's cost per fight is above what one process holds. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The forager wakes to a returner's basket, and carving a name on the stone is choosing the player's name
## 2026-10-25 ⏸ The forager wakes to a returner's basket, and carving a name on the stone is choosing the player's name

Superseded the same day by "A bell calls every new returner to the stone": a found trace that one player keeps leaves nothing for the next.

The owner read the west bank fix of #327 told as the player lives it (pick the herbs in the shallows, find the bridge's missing stone under them, strike it) and answered: the story is bad. Three story-first versions were written (a name in the basket's handle, the mist stepping back from each picked herb, a stone knocking in the shallows); the owner picked the first, with a change.
- **The basket by the forager's path carries a name cut in its handle**, and its dry herbs smell of the shallows by the bridge: the trace leads to the herbs over the keystone (#327's build), the strike mends the bridge, and on the returners' stone beside it the same name stands among the others above the empty line. The question it leaves (whoever cut that name mended this bridge once - why is the basket here and its owner not?) is chapter 1's open question at minute 1. The handle and the stone speak; the narrator states no past (S1).
- **The owner's change: the names on the stone are the names of players who signed in**, not six written names, and carving one's own on the empty line is the moment the player chooses their name. Chapter 1 already planned real names with accounts (beat 2; "with accounts in stage 2, real players' names"); this makes the name screen a scene in the world.
- Until a server answers (no account, offline, or too few players), the stone shows the written names already in the content. A name shown to other players passes the same filter as any player text (the safety contract), and the player's chosen name is what carves.
- **Every start passes through the same moment** (the owner, the same day): the digger's and the explorer's openings share the forager's weakness (no one, no thread) and are rewritten as a set on the same thread - one returner's name found in each pocket (the forager's basket handle, a name on the digger's buried axe, a name on the bridge post by the explorer's axe), each opening ending at the stone on a name the player already read, where every player carves the name they choose.
**Retry when:** the stone's names cannot be read from the server at the opening (then the written names stay until they can), or the owner wants the name chosen before the world loads. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ⏸ The progression map: the long game's spine, schools, gear and unlock economy, as a decision page

The owner, 25.10: the game's infrastructure is that of the big long games (Fallout, Skyrim), very long to complete, levelling and crafted gear, clothing and weapons all tied to skills that unlock. `docs/design/PROGRESSION.md` turns that into world rules and algorithms against what is decided and built: rise-by-use opens, points buy (the hybrid the kernel already holds, sized long); one experience curve for every school on the game's own difficulty ratio (1.5), twenty steps, mastery at 21; bands of five levels that gate nodes and never verbs; the character level read by three things only (the power cap by formula, the slots re-spaced, travel); a catalogue of sixteen schools in five families with each school's closed effect list; a school that grows by branches to about 550 nodes at the first full catalogue and a thousand by data; gear with a tier, a rarity and a third axis, quality, that a node opens while no node walls an attempt; the unlock economy's four prices (point, band, key, find) and nothing for gold; proposed rule lines P1-P9; a conflict table (one declared conflict: cross-links between schools against the 23.10 format's "same school only"); twenty holes closed by analogy.
Nothing is built with it. Eight calls are the owner's, in the page's section 9, each with a recommended default: Q1 how long "complete" is (default `t1` 15 s: one school in about 28 active hours, sixteen in about 450), Q2 combat schools reserved now and written with the first foes, Q3 the hybrid kept, Q4 respec limited (three free, then gold), Q5 attribute pips after the opening kept, Q6 quests pay a named school, Q7 a lesson is time and never gold for a level, Q8 a cold clock.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ A story keeper runs from the first pitch of a quest, and CI asks for its verdict on any story text

The owner, 25.10, after a west bank fix whose mechanics passed every check and whose story read badly: an agent with a trigger in the system should surface where the story stops making sense, send it to the manager for an answer, and write a story fix only after approval - and it belongs inside the writing of a quest from the start, not after.
Built: the story-keeper skill (`.claude/skills/story-keeper`) with nine questions (who says it, can they know it, why do they want it, has it happened yet for this player, is it still true in the game, does it contradict a decision, is the thread whole, who is the player, what does the next player find). The quest-designer runs it on every pitch (a `Story:` line; a pitch with an open story question goes to the manager before the owner sees the pitches) and on the built file. The trigger: `tools/src/pr-rules.ts` owes a `Story review:` line in the body of any pull request that changes text a player reads as story (quests, NPCs, pockets, restores, reactions, treasures, races, STORY.md and the chapter pages), in the same CI job as the quest review and the picture's source. A finding that adds a fact waits for the manager's or the owner's answer, and the answer goes into DECISIONS.md the same day.
Why a skill and a body line, not a judge in CI: CI runs no model; the line makes the review impossible to skip, the same way `Quest review:` does.
**Retry when:** CI can run a model on a pull request (then the keeper's questions can run there as a second eye), or the line is met by rote verdicts with findings later found in play (then the questions need a check that measures them). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Fishing: a rod, spots on the water, and a cast in separable states

The owner, 25.10: a full fishing action - rod and fish items; fishing spots on water; fish as dark silhouettes under the surface; an elastic line that lands with a bobbing float; when a fish takes it the line pulls taut and a reel plays; cast, bite and reel kept as separable states.
Built: fishing is its own profession, `core/fishing` (verb `fish`), with its own school `core/fishing-school` (still water, patient line, quick reel, deep pool; corners for the human, the elf and the skeleton), every node on effects from the closed list (`timing_pct`, `gather_speed_pct`, `key_senses`); no new effect was added. A node fished with `tool: "rod"` trains fishing, as a node felled with `axe` trains woodcutting, and stays the kernel's `gather` action. A resource node may say `place.water: true`: its instances stand on water tiles beside land the player can stand on, so the player fishes from the bank. The river fish swim by the west bank (`core/river-fishing`, three spots). The rod is made at the workshop (two branches and a reed cord, known from the start); raw fish is a material, grilled at the fire into a meal.
The minigame is `fish`: the float flies out (`cast`), rides the water (`wait`), twitches on one to three nibbles (`nibble`), goes under (`bite`) and comes back on the strike (`reel`), or the fish is lost (`lost`). One press: before the bite frightens the fish off, after the window it lets go; inside, sooner is better. The bite always follows the last nibble by the same tell (520 ms), so the jackpot (a strike inside the first 124 ms, faster than a reaction) goes to a player who reads the nibble; the window widens with the player, the jackpot strip does not. Each state is told to the world, which draws in code with the palette: a wooden rod in the hand, a quadratic line that springs when the float lands, sags while waiting and goes taut on the bite, a red and white float with rings where it lands and where the fish takes it, and dark fish shapes circling under every spot that has fish (the hooked one holds under the float). The 3D door shows the minigame without the line.
Stand-ins: the fish icon is the stone's, the rod's the branch's, the grilled fish the mushroom skewer's - existing art only, until fish art is drawn; they pass the rich rule as reused refs. The rig's fishing clips (cast, wait, bite, reel) are guides for frames not drawn yet; until then the arm swing marks the cast and the reel.
**Retry when:** fish art is drawn (then the stand-ins go), the fishing frames are drawn over the rig's guides (then the player casts and reels with them), or play shows the nibble tell is too easy or too hard to read (then `FISH_TELL_MS` or the jackpot strip moves). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-25 ✅ Stone is mined: blows that run by themselves, as felling, training digging

The owner, 25.10: mining stone should feel like felling - strikes, the rock answering, a yield - and extend an existing action rather than add a near-duplicate.
Built: a resource node may say `"work": "mine"`. Such a node is still the kernel's `gather` action, worked by one press with the blows running by themselves (the client's `mine` kind, the same rhythm as felling), with no minigame. It needs no tool: bare hands mine, so no race and no opening is locked out. A pick or a hammer in the bag breaks it faster (the best one by tier, +150 permille per tier inside the power cap) and wears as any tool does. The river stones are mined; the keystone keeps the strike minigame the opening teaches, so this replaces the 2026-10-11 line "the stones and the keystone keep the strike minigame" for the river stones only. The rock answers: each blow strikes with dust and sparks, chips grow with the work and a crack sounds past the middle; the last blow splits the stone with a crack, chips thrown wide and a small jolt (2D; the 3D door gets the strike and the crack).
Mining trains Digging, not a new profession: stone and earth are the same hands' work, and a sixth profession would be a near-duplicate of digging with a different tool. The digging school gets two seed nodes for it, "Stone breaker" (small) and "Rock grain" (major), both on `gather_speed_pct`, the effect a mined node reads (as the woodcutting school does for felling); no new effect was added. The pose sheet falls back to the axe swing (`POSES.mine` tries `pick` first) until the mine frames are drawn over the rig's guides.
**Retry when:** the mine frames are drawn (then `pick` is used), a deeper rock or ore node lands (then a pick may become required for it with `tool: "pick"`), or play shows players read stone as digging poorly (then mining becomes its own profession with its own school). Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-25 ✅ A bell calls every new returner to the stone, the name comes first, and the tools come after

The owner caught a break in the found-trace opening of the entry above: if the digger takes a former player's axe, what does the next player find, and where did the axe go? A shared valley repeats every start, so a trace one player keeps cannot also wait for the next (the story keeper's ninth question since). The owner rejected a step-by-step version as a chore and corrected a story version into this one:
- **Each start wakes in its own place** (the north meadow, the slid bank, the west bank). A bell rings once from the river, and a quest window opens: "Follow the sound."
- **The stone by the broken bridge carries a ghost bell.** A few seconds after the player arrives it rings, animated. It rings for every new player who signs in, so every player in the valley hears a far bell when a new returner arrives.
- **At the stone the player only decides who they are**: they carve their name on the empty line under the other players' names. No tool waits at the stone.
- **After the carve, a quest by race finds the player's own tool and a hammer.** The hammer is a new tool every player gets: each race's hammer is drawn for that race, with small fixed differences in effect; it takes no slot and is never swapped.
- **The bridge is mended with them, and nobody is waiting on the other side.** The player decides where to go from there.
The stone's names follow the entry above (players who signed in, the written names until a server answers; the safety filter). Kept from it: carving is the only naming, for every start.
**Retry when:** the owner changes the opening, or the bell for every signed-in player is too loud for a busy valley (then it rings only within a distance or a rate). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map, Q5: pips bought to 5, and worn gear adds attribute bonuses on top, with a soft wear requirement (the owner, 25.10)

PROGRESSION.md Q5, options A (fixed for life), B (pips bought with points to 5; recommended) and C (pips from gear only). The owner's word (25.10): **B, and gear too**.
- Pips are bought with points up to `key_max` 5, as built.
- Worn gear adds attribute bonuses on top. They scale with the item's tier and have **no fixed cap**: late gear must match late stages (his amendment the same evening, replacing a first reading with a cap of the base plus three).
- The stats panel shows the total with the gear part in another colour, for example "Might 7 = 5 + 2 gear"; an item's description lists its attribute bonuses under its name.
- **The wear requirement**: each item states one, a school level and an attribute. Below it the item is still worn and still helps, gently: its bonus counts up to what gear one tier above the wearer's own tier gives, and the rest unlocks as the wearer meets the requirement. The tooltip shows the full bonus, the active part and what unlocks the rest ("+6 might, 3 active, the rest at Guard 30" in his words; on the twenty-step curve the level shown is the tier's level on 1-21). His reason: a newcomer who saved and bought from another player gets a real step ahead, not the late game.
- Premium looks never carry a bonus (power is never sold).
The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 5.6): `attr_bonus_max` by tier [1, 2, 3, 4, 6], then +2 a tier; the requirement's level on the band ladder (tier 2 level 6 up to tier 5 mastery); a requirement and a node's key read the base attributes (race, grade, pips), never worn bonuses, so gear cannot climb through gear and keys never become a shopping list; the same one-tier-ahead rule reads the item's worn effects.
**Retry when:** the free market makes one tier ahead the normal way to play (then the step narrows, never the rule), or a late stage needs a bonus the tier ladder cannot reach. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map, Q7: teachers give time and ask standing; help in a group, never a carry (the owner, 25.10)

PROGRESSION.md Q7, options A (a lesson is time at the ordinary rate, asking standing; recommended), B (teachers sell levels for gold) and C (books only). The owner's word (25.10): **A**, never gold for a level. And no industrial carry, above all in multiplayer; help is welcome, a full carry is not (his amendment the same evening):
- Experience comes only from the player's own actions (active ms are already per player); a quest done beside a veteran pays the newcomer for what the newcomer did.
- In a group with a stronger player, a newcomer gets (a) a share of the group's earned experience, at most a quarter of their own, bounded by their own active time, and (b) +20% on their own earned experience while the veteran is near (Final Fantasy XIV's mentors, World of Warcraft's rested experience).
- The veteran earns standing and a line on the giving board (the boards, 9.10), not experience.
- Diminishing returns for the same pair.
- Selling carries falls under "An exploit loop costs its accounts and the gold it made" (9.10).
The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 3.7): the terms per school, so a quarter of nothing is nothing; "stronger" one band above in that school, "near" the same group within 12 tiles; the pair's help full for its first 60 active minutes in a real day, half for the next 60, none after, reset at 00:00 UTC. The +20% is declared as the one multiplier on experience (against R6's activity record); no body state or node adds another.
**Superseded in part (26.10):** the group share is a split of experience the group already earned, moved from the earners and never made; the mentor's +20% stays the one bonus (the ✅ entry "Balance prover finding 2" below; PROGRESSION 3.7).
**Retry when:** pairs or rings of accounts farm the share (then the pair schedule shortens and the exploit-loop rule acts), or newcomers in groups still fall behind solo players (then the share, never a carry). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map, Q8a: a harsh place slows and takes health by its grade (the owner, 25.10)

The Q8 answer left open whether a harsh place also costs health; the recommended default was slow only, with health loss in one or two end-game places. A calm snowy place costs about a point every few seconds, tolerable for a while; harsher places cost more; the right gear cancels the cost, fully or one grade down.

Bare, from full: 4 min 10 s at grade 1 (a new player loses a point every 2.5 s: calm snow), 2 min 23 s, 1 min 23 s, 50 s at grade 4; a mid player (a tier 3-4 trait) has no drain up to grade 2, 4 min 10 s at grade 3; grade 4 only in one or two places at the end of the long way. A safe place (G30) stops the drain; one warning box an outing and a countdown at the edge (G31); signs and counts, never colour alone or a percentage (G29, decision 39); at zero the fall of G33; nothing runs while away (G28).
Declared: G32's "Nothing drains it by the clock (G28)" bends: the drain is the place's, never the body's, and runs only while the player stands in an unanswered grade.
**Retry when:** grade 1 kills new players who did not read the warning (then a slower grade 1, or the countdown sooner), or the drain is ignored because food out-heals it (then the ladder, never a meter). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map, Q8b: food heals, cooked dishes give timed effects, sleep only saves the night (the owner, 25.10)

The Q8 answer left open whether Fed and Rested survive as plain timed buffs; the recommended default was to keep them.
