## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map, balance: two red lines, rising ceilings, walls proven both ways (the owner, 25.10)

The owner, on the balance proof he held back: hard fixed caps would kill the chase for better sets, and fully open stacking lets an unbeatable combination exist; and the end-game's places and quests should be so hard that only near-end players pass them, while to everyone else they feel like an impossible wall. His model, approved the same day:
1. **Two red lines, absolute.** (a) No loop: no source may amplify another source that amplifies it back; the effect graph is acyclic and an effect never raises the strength of the effect kinds that raised it (Skyrim's Fortify Restoration potions and enchanted gear, which reached damage in the millions); craft, sell, buy and repeat nets at most 0 at a vendor. (b) Nothing grants experience except the mentor's +20%.
2. **Rising ceilings instead of fixed caps.** Each stat category has a ceiling by tier band (Diablo II's and Path of Exile's 75% resistance cap, raised by rare late items, to 90% in Path of Exile). Sets, dishes and traits combine freely, and are meant to, up to the band's ceiling. A small closed family of **ceiling raisers**, rare, late and few, each raises one category's ceiling by a step: the end-game chase.
3. **Walls proven both ways.** A place or quest flagged as a wall has a band; the check proves it **solvable** (an explicit witness build, recorded with the content, at the wall's band passes it) and **not cheesable** (no combination below band wall - 1 passes it, enumerated over the whole catalogue below that band). Pass is numeric per kind: health drain against sustain over the wall's length, the slow against the timer, the attribute and school requirement.
4. The check enumerates and reports the red lines, the ceilings and both wall proofs, and, as a warning only, the top builds per band, so designers see when the strongest runs too far above the median. Spec only: a check-row family in the `catalogue_hours_min` style; the script is a follow-up, not built in this pull request.
5. The rule "no combination answers more than one grade above the wearer's tier" is dropped; the walls replace it.
The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 4.7, BODY_CLOCKS.md 5.3, 6.2 and 7): a made thing's strength, quality, tier and duration read only its recipe, its inputs' tier, the maker's tree and base levels, never worn gear or a dish (worn `craft_speed_pct` and `craft_chance_pct` change speed and success, not strength). Seven categories with ceilings for bands 1-5: `work` 25-65% off (two raisers of 5 to 75%, the power cap's 4x), `move` 10-30% (2 x 5), `health` +10-50% above the attributes' cap (2 x 10), `sustain` 5-15% of the cap a game hour (1 x 5), `find` +3 to +15 (2 x 3), `sense` +1 to +4 tiles (1 x 1), `place` 2, 2, 3, 3, 4 steps (no raiser; the ladder tops at 4); combat's three reserved. Ten raisers, half crown nodes and half unique band-5 finds, never sold or made, bound. The wearer's band in a category is their Q5 own tier in its governing school (Survival for health, sustain, sense and place; Travel for move). Attributes stay outside the ceilings (Q5). `sustain` is enforced in play inside a draining grade (health back, food included, at most at that rate). Pass: T = length x slow x (1 - speed off); health at the end = cap - T x (drain - sustain) above 0, the witness above `wall_margin_bp` 1000; T within the timer; requirements on base levels, refusing nothing in play (the chance falls to nothing, as a recipe's, 9.10). Rows: `effect_graph_acyclic`, `loop_profit_max` 0, `xp_sources` mentor, `ceiling_<category>` with raise step and count, `wall_witness_required`, `wall_margin_bp` 1000, `wall_cheese_gap` 2, `top_builds_n` 5 and `top_over_median_max_permille` 1600 (warnings), `proof_enum_max` 10,000,000. Worked example: a band-5 pass at grade 4 frost and storm, 12 game hours: the band-5 witness passes untouched, band 3 loses 121% of the cap, band 4 comes through with a sixth.
**Supersedes:** the ⏸ entry "The progression map: the balance proof (pending)" (above), and the Q8 fill's "never more than one grade above the wearer's own tier". Declared: G32's "eating is how the player heals" bends inside a draining grade only (the `sustain` clamp).
**Superseded in part (25.10):** the governing school of a ceiling's band (Survival for health) is replaced by the ceiling owners: health from the stamina attribute, `sustain`, `sense` and `place` from Survival, `move` and a new `carry` from Travel (the ✅ entry "The progression map: who owns each ceiling" below).
**Retry when:** the top-builds warning fires on most bands (then the ceilings or the sources, never a fixed cap), a wall's witness is the only build that passes it at its band (then the wall is softened toward a family of builds), or a cycle appears in the effect graph (then the edge is removed, never the check). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map: common things are plain (the owner, 25.10)

The owner's rule (25.10): the base rarity, **common**, for food and for accessories and gear, carries **no effect, or one very small one, never two**.
The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 5.7, BODY_CLOCKS.md 6.2 and 7.3): the four rarities the schema has (`common`, `uncommon`, `rare`, `legendary`, 9.10) stay, orthogonal to tier as the schema states: tier sets the strength row and the ceiling band, rarity how many effects a thing may carry and where in its tier's row they sit (common at most 1, at most the stat's smallest step: a worn effect's 5, +1 to an attribute, 1 grade, a dish effect's tier-1 value; uncommon 1 at 50-75%; rare 2 at 75-100%; legendary 2 at 100%, and the found ceiling raisers). Quality moves a piece inside its rarity's band; a master piece's second worn effect needs rare or above (amending the page's own 5.2). A dish's rarity is that of the rarest ingredient taking part in one of its effects, capped by the cook's Cooking band (1: uncommon, 2-3: rare, 4-5: legendary); a dish of common ingredients only is common and shows at most one effect. Garment traits exist at common only at strength 1, so a common wool hood and common snowshoes, made at tier 1 in the valley, still answer calm snow (grade 1) fully. Check rows `common_plain`, `rarity_effects_max` [1, 1, 2, 2] and `rarity_band_permille`, spec only; today's content already passes them (every built common piece carries 0 or 1 worn effect at 5).
Open, an owner call (PROGRESSION section 9): whether an attribute bonus counts as a common piece's one effect (recommended: it counts; the Q8 entry's "every garment keeps the tree points it adds" then holds from uncommon up).
**Retry when:** commons feel like junk nobody makes (then a common's one effect is chosen better, never a second), or uncommon supply is so thin that grade 2 places stall (then the rarity budget, never stronger commons). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ The progression map: who owns each ceiling (the owner, 25.10)

The owner (25.10): the health ceiling comes from the existing stamina attribute (health = 100 + 20 a stamina point; Fallout's split, where Endurance sets hit points); Survival sets health back, the senses and the answer to places; Travel sets the walking speed, and Travel must carry real weight as a school (the next entry).

| Stat category (PROGRESSION 4.7) | Owner | The band it reads |
|---|---|---|
| `health` | the stamina attribute | the base stamina, at least 1 (0 or 1 is band 1, 5 is band 5); the cap itself stays 100 + 20 a point |
| `sustain` (health back while a place drains) | Survival | the own tier in Survival (Q5) |
| `sense` | Survival | the same |
| `place` (the answer to a place) | Survival | the same |
| `move` | Travel | the own tier in Travel |
| `carry` (new) | Travel | the same |
| `work` | the school at work | its own tier |
| `find` | the gathering school in use | its own tier |
| `strike`, `aim`, `guard` | their own schools, with combat | reserved |

The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 4.7): a band is read on base levels and base pips as Q5 reads them, so worn gear never lifts its own ceiling; an attribute's own bonuses stay outside the ceilings, and stamina only picks which band of the health ceiling applies; a new `carry` category (+10% to +30% of the weight limit by band, no raisers) holds Travel's load nodes. Fallout: New Vegas splits it the same way: Endurance sets hit points, the Survival skill sets what food heals.
**Supersedes in part:** the balance entry's sentence on the governing school (Survival for health), marked there.
**Retry when:** stamina becomes the one pip every build buys first (then the health band from Survival, as first drafted, never a larger value a point), or Survival feels empty without health (then a Survival node that answers a drain, never the cap). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Travel carries weight: a full school of carrying, ways over, water and the sense of a route (the owner, 25.10)

The owner (25.10): Travel is far too empty as a skill and must carry real weight: a full school with a real 51.7 h path in the #302 node format with closed effects, built from what exists or is planned in the repository with each source cited; nodes that wait on systems not built yet are marked, their places reserved; the 800 h catalogue check still holds.
The builder's fill (proposed, PROGRESSION 4.8):
1. **Five parts, each from the repository.** *Carrying*, from the races-and-bags pull request (#297: the weight limit, three paces, bags on the back): Travel raises the limit (`load_pct`, under the new `carry` ceiling), closes part of the heavy pace's 40 ms (`load_step_pct`), lends a second belt tool and an edge over the hard limit (`edge_pct`). *Ways over*, RuneScape's Agility shortcuts on the hand-made map (G61, NEXT 19b's shortcut, G68's rock faces, 19a and 19c's height layer): stones, a log and a squeeze from level 1, a ford from 6, a scramble and a ledge from 11, a leap from 16; Climbing keeps the rope drops of two or three levels; the long way round always stays (`shortcut_reach_closed`), so P3 holds with G61's exception. *Fords*: wading a river at a ford mark. *Water*: the boathouse's lent rowboat (19f) and oar nodes on the stroke; an own boat and the boat line stay forms of the character level (3.5). *Route sense*: a wider chart reveal and a read trail (`path_read`, `map_wide`, `way_home`).
2. **What pays Travel:** crossings, fords and rowing, trail courses, quests and lessons, and (an open call) the time a heavy load adds to a step. A plain step pays nothing, and one action pays one school (`xp_one_school`), so no hour counts twice.
3. **The path:** 32 nodes (a main line of 12, three race corners, the Trail and Pack branches of eight, the crown `wayfarer`, which raises `move`); 25 wait on systems not built yet (#297, NEXT 19a, 19c and 19f, the row verb, the sled), 7 on nothing but their own line. At 28 s a first level and ratio 1.5: band 2 at 6.2 min, band 3 at 53 min, band 4 at 6.8 h, the crown at 51.7 h.
4. **The catalogue still holds:** the first full catalogue's sixteen schools (Travel among them) at 28 s are 827 h, past `catalogue_hours_min` (800); all twenty-two are 1,138 h.
5. **The stacking rule with Q8a's places:** Travel never lowers a grade. A garment or a dish answers the grade, and Travel quickens the step: `step_ms = pace * slow * (100 - move) / 100`, where `slow` comes from the grade left after the answer and `move` stops at the Travel band's ceiling; one dish effect is either a place answer or a move effect, never both; the slow applies after the clamp. So Travel and gear never cancel the same slow twice (BODY_CLOCKS.md 5.6).
Corrected on the way: section 4.3's point count (48 a school, 42 a player's share; the first count doubled the branches), and the count of schools made exact (twenty-two in the tables, sixteen in the first full catalogue).
Open, owner calls (PROGRESSION section 9): does a heavy step pay Travel (recommended: yes, only the added time); trail courses (recommended: yes, one a band).
**Retry when:** walking heavy becomes the cheapest Travel training (then the heavy step pays nothing), a shortcut feels like a wall (then its level comes down, never fewer long ways round), or a place needs both Travel and a garment to cross (then the stacking rule is checked, never a third source). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Balance prover finding 1: the berry-mash vendor loop breaks red line A (proposed: off the stall, or a higher price)

The balance prover (`pnpm balance`, #342) run on content: buy two raw berries at the trader (5,000 mg each), make berry mash (4 s, level 1, a sure craft), sell it for 31,500 mg: +21,500 mg a craft, a money loop the 4.7 red line A forbids (craft, sell, buy and repeat nets at most 0).
Proposed value: take raw berries off the trader's stall, or price a berry at no less than 15,750 mg (half the mash's sale price). Effect: `loop_profit_max` passes on content and can then be wired into `pnpm check`; gathering berries stays the only way into mash money.
**Accepted 2026-10-26, as proposed:** a raw berry costs 15,750 mg at the trader's stall (`content/core/shops/trader-shop.json`), half the mash's sale price, so a craft nets 0 and `loop_profit_max` passes on content; the other form, berries off the stall, would break the recorded openings and the simulated player, which buy a berry there.
**Retry when:** the owner picks one of the two (then a ✅ entry, the content change and the row wired). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Balance prover finding 2: the group share (Q7) is an experience source beside the mentor (proposed: it splits experience already earned)

Red line B (4.7: nothing grants experience except the mentor's +20%) against Q7's group rule: a newcomer's share of the group's earned experience (at most a quarter of their own) adds experience nobody earned, so it is a second source.
Proposed value: the share is a split of experience the group already earned (moved from the earners, never created); the mentor's +20% stays the only bonus. Effect: `xp_sources` holds for groups; a carry stays bounded as Q7 meant, and the sum of a group's experience equals its members' own work.
**Accepted 2026-10-26, as proposed:** PROGRESSION 3.7 words the share as moved from the earners, never made; the Q7 entry carries a superseding note.

A uniform zoom keeps every ratio, so the mismatch is the character's scale against the world, not the zoom. Measured on the kit's sprites (the opaque height at the sprite's own density; a tile is 16 world px) and in the game at the slid bank's dig spot:

| Prop | Today, tiles | Against the character (1.5 tiles) | STYLE.md 1b wants, against the character |
|---|---|---|---|
| The cut-out character, the old player sprite, Pell | 1.5, 1.44, 1.44 | 1, 0.96, 0.96 | 1 (the frame, 1 x 1.5 tiles) |
| Maru | 1.31 | 0.87 | 0.9, stooped |
| Oak | 2.0 | 1.33 | at least 3 (3 x 5 tiles) |
| Young tree | 1.44 | 0.96 | 2 (2 x 3 tiles) |
| Dead tree | 2.0 | 1.33 | 2.5 (3 x 4 tiles) |
| Lantern post | 2.0 | 1.33 | 2 (1 x 3 tiles) |
| Standing stone | 1.81 | 1.21 | 1.5 to 2 |
| Small house | 3.0 | 2.0 | 3 (3 x 5 tiles) |
| Its door (the opening) | 0.875 | 0.58 | about 1.2 (112 px of 64 a tile) |
| Bush | 0.88 | 0.59 | 0.5 |
| Rocks and stones | 0.58 to 0.83 | 0.39 to 0.55 | - |
| Dig spot (the loose earth) | 0.53, one tile wide | 0.35 | - |

The character matches STYLE.md 1b's frame and today's player and people, and the ground things (bushes, rocks, the dig spot, items) already sit at their table size. The odd ones out are the tall props: trees, posts, stones and the house are still at the 16 px grid's sizes, 1.5 to 2.5 times shorter than the table, and a door is about half a character tall. A second round of stills set the character at 1, 1.25 and 1.5 times against one still with the tall props grown to the table, and the owner approved the recommendation:
- **The camera comes closer:** the game's own whole zoom step at or above 1.5 times the screen's own zoom (`4 * ceil(1.5 * base / 4)`: 8 to 12 on a 390x844 phone at 3x, 1.5 times; 4 to 8 on a 1440x900 desktop at 1x, 2 times), so an art pixel stays a whole number of device pixels.
- **The character is the measure, at 1x:** never grown; each body at its rig height ratio (human man 84, human woman 80, elf man 88, elf woman 84, skeleton 76).
- **The tall props grow to the STYLE 1b table:** the owner approved the table's numbers as the target (STYLE 1b held its numbers until one look; this is that look).

Judged frames: [2026-10-25-size-phone-2.png](docs/design/frames/2026-10-25-size-phone-2.png) (the camera closer, the human man idle by the slid bank; the pick) and the second round's still of the approved recommendation, [2026-10-25-size-phone-w.png](docs/design/frames/2026-10-25-size-phone-w.png) and [2026-10-25-size-desktop-w.png](docs/design/frames/2026-10-25-size-desktop-w.png) (the camera closer, the character at 1x digging at the slid bank's dig spot, the tall props grown to about the table in the page for the comparison only; from that spot the grown oak, 5 tiles, is the tall prop in view).
Both doors: the proportions hold in the 3D door as ratios to the character's height (STYLE.md, both doors); the zoom step is the classic client's.
**Follow-ups** (NEXT `camera-closer` and `tall-props-to-table`; nothing is built here):
- (a) the real client's camera zoom set to the picked view, and the e2e camera test (`game/e2e/camera.ts`, zoom 8 at a 3x phone today) updated to it;
- (b) the tall props redrawn or regrown to the table. All of them are on the pre-rich redraw list (#328, `docs/art/PRE_RICH_REDRAW.md`): the canopies (row 7), the oak, the young tree and the dead tree (row 11), the standing stone (row 14), the small house, the well, the market stall and the lantern post (row 22), and the ruin arch, wall and pillar, the statues and the totem (row 28). A nearest-neighbour upscale of the 16 px art is only a stopgap; the rich redraw at the table's sizes is the real fix.

## 2026-10-26 ✅ The health bar is named hp, and the hunger drain is gone from the code

NEXT 1, item 7, after crafting wave 4 landed (#86). The bar the 2026-10-08 entry made into health kept its food-bar name: `player.satiety` in milli-points, kept lazily against `player.satietyAt` for a drain by the clock that the core set to 0 at P-5c (11.10) and that G28 forbids. The builder's choice (technical, so ✅), as GRUNT P-5 planned it: the state holds one number, `player.hp`, with no tick beside it; its top is `hpCap` (`params.hp_cap`, the old `satiety_cap`, plus stamina); `satiety_drain_per_min` leaves the params, the schema and the item text ("about 2 min of work" can no longer be shown); the `eat` event reports `hp`, as `hurt` already did. `KERNEL_VERSION` 0.19.0, and `migrateState` carries a saved satiety over as hp and drops the old fields, so every save still loads, with the value it had, since nothing drained. Replay is unchanged for the shipped content, which drained nothing. Kept as it is: the content field `food.satiety` (how many points one food gives back), because renaming a field creators write is not additive and `FORMAT_VERSION` stays 1; its description already says health.
**Retry when:** the content format moves to version 2 (then `food.satiety` becomes `food.heal`, as GRUNT P-5 named it), or a world needs hunger again (then it comes back as its own bar, never as a drain on health, G28). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Tree first, both ways: every feature takes its place in the skill tree, and every quest and task starts from it (the owner, 25.10)

The owner's rule, standing from today. **The tree from the work:** every new mechanic, stat, effect, item property or system that a player can grow in is placed in the skill tree when it is designed, either on an existing school's nodes or in a proposal that extends the tree (a new branch or node group, in the school file's node format of #302). The call lands on the developer: whoever builds the thing makes it, and it must be visible in review. **The work from the tree:** when there is a quest or a task, the agents propose from the skills. An agent starting any task reads the tree first and says, before it builds, where the work sits or which nodes it serves; the quest designer reads the tree first, starts from its thin spots, and every pitch names the schools it trains and the nodes it needs or opens.
Made enforceable, not prose:
- **A fourth body line, checked by CI** in the same job as `Quest review:`, `Story review:` and `Source:` (#331): `Tree: <school>/<node ids>`, `Tree: extends the tree: <path of the proposal>`, or `Tree: none: <why nothing a player can grow in changes>`. Unlike the other three it is owed by every pull request, since any change may grow the player. A bare "none", a "none:" with no reason, the template's placeholder and a line in none of the three forms are refused (`tools/src/pr-rules.ts`, tested in `tools/test/pr-rules.test.ts`).
- **Where agents read it:** AGENTS.md ("How work moves", and the quest designer's row in the stage table), the quest-designer skill (read first, the pitch's seventh line, the build's step 3b) and the pull request template.
- **The tree's thin spots, as a report (spec only, a follow-up):** a read-only script, proposed name `pnpm tree:coverage`, that lists per school the nodes with no content that touches them: no quest that pays the school or asks for the node's band, no recipe of the school's profession at the node's band, no place, crossing or teacher that opens or trains it. Reserved nodes (those that wait on a system not built) are listed first, with the system they wait on. It informs and never fails; the quest designer reads it first. The full spec is in PROGRESSION.md section 4.9 (#333).
Why a body line and not a check on the content: CI cannot tell what a player grows in from a diff; the line makes the builder say it, and the reviewer reads it, the same way the quest and story lines work.
**Retry when:** the line is met by rote ("none: small fix" on changes that did grow the player) and a review finds it; then the coverage report gains a row that compares the diff's effect names, items and recipes against the line. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-25 ✅ Characters are made industrially

The owner, 25.10, after three animation trials: a character is made by a pipeline, not drawn pose by pose. The look follows the 3D models' proportions, as in the trial's forager, which the owner rated excellent.
The route and its stages:
1. **A 3D model per character** on a known skeleton (the cast of `game/public3d/models/`, Mixamo bone names).
2. **Library motion:** a clip of the Quaternius Universal Animation Library 1 or 2 retargeted onto the model; where the libraries have no clip, an IK overlay on a library base (the dig and the harvest's reach).
3. **A Blender render snapped to the palette:** three views (down, up, left; right is the left mirrored), every pixel snapped to the shared ramps, a flat ID pass for flame, tool metal and leaves, the one outline.
4. **Automated checks:** `pnpm anim render` and `pnpm anim check` (palette, outline, silhouette, foot slide and the poses a second each pace needs), exit 1 on a failure.
5. **The owner sees only new or exceptional work:** a contact sheet across the cast and the list of exceptions.
Sheets enter `content/` only after the owner has seen the trial-3 contact sheet across the whole cast.

Measured (scratch trials; the tool is `tools/anim`, the pull request that carries this entry):
- Trial 1 (one body, three clips, a scratch script): the first clip 25 min of setup and work with 24 s of compute, the second 5 min and 52 s, the third 2 min and 43 s; palette 100 %, foot slide 0.41 px.
- Trial 2 (both libraries, 14 clips on the forager and on the hand-keyed rig): every clip palette 100 % and outline on every frame; 17-49 s of machine time a clip with the repo tool; a new harvest asked for after review was on disk 22-25 min later. Pace: at the game's 337 px/s a distance-locked walk needs 55-105 poses a second (over the screen's 30); the jog (14-25) and the sprint (13-23) fit.
- Trial 3 (the cast, 10 models x 11 clips x 3 views, one command per body): 88 of 110 clips rendered (the two Kenney minis refused by the tool's skeleton preflight, about 3 s a clip); 46 of 110 pass every check, 61 of the 88 with the walk's known stride limit aside; 38 min of machine time (35 in Blender), about 4.6 min per new character (11 clips x 3 views, median of eight, 4.2-5.9) plus a target entry whose two ramps a script picks from the model's colour map, and about 3.5 min per new action across the eight bodies. Seven of the eight Meshy bodies retarget cleanly; the mage's skeleton was transferred from the warrior, not fitted, and breaks (the checks failed 8 of its 11 clips and missed 3). Exceptions on the clean bodies: frames sized on the forager cut the taller bodies (13 clips), the walk's stride (22), feet sliding just over 1 px (5), long coats and dresses webbing between the legs at full stride, and a small accent colour lost to the two-ramp rule.
Licences and credit lines:
- Universal Animation Library [Standard] and Universal Animation Library 2 [Standard], by Quaternius: CC0 1.0 (`License.txt` in each archive), fetched by sha256 (`tools/anim/libraries.json`), never committed.
- The cast's Meshy models: CC BY 4.0 under section 3.2 of the Meshy Terms of Use. Every sheet rendered from one is a derivative and carries that model's credit line from `game/public3d/models/README.md` plus "posed with library motion and rendered to pixels by tools/anim" (each target's `credit` in `tools/anim/anim.json`, written into every strip's json).
- The Kenney Mini Characters are CC0 but have a 7-bone toy rig; the pipeline refuses them.
- Quaternius releases new packs under the Quaternius Asset License v1.0 (28.8.2026: free use in a game, no credit, no redistribution of the assets themselves). A QAL asset may feed the pipeline, but is fetched by hash like the libraries and never committed to this public repository.
The hand-keyed rig keeps one role: a reference for attach points (hand, back and head sockets) and for layers (what lies in front of what in each view). It is not a motion source.
The cut-out route stays a demo and a cheap fallback for NPC variety.
NEXT (follow-ups, not built here):
- (a) Engine: pick the pose from the distance walked (`travel_px_per_pose` in each strip's json), and use the jog for tile moves at the current speed.
- (b) The sheets into `content/kit` after the owner's look at trial 3, with the kit's art checks extended to the new 3D-rendered sheets.
- (c) The cast gaps, an elf and a skeleton: no ready elf and no CC0 skeleton on the library's skeleton. Quaternius' Universal Base Characters (https://quaternius.itch.io/universal-base-characters, CC0, free tier two bodies) carry all 65 of the library's bones, so the motion plays on them with no map; an elf is one of them in the CC0 ranger outfit (https://quaternius.itch.io/modular-character-outfits-fantasy) with ears weighted to the head, or, keeping the cast's look, a Meshy model fitted with `game/tools/blender/person_rig.py` like the six people. A skeleton: KayKit Skeletons (https://kaylousberg.itch.io/kaykit-skeletons, CC0, free tier four skeletons on a 23-bone rig) need a third map and the renderer keeping every skinned mesh; Bestiary - Dungeon Monsters Kit (https://quaternius.itch.io/bestiary-dungeon-monsters-kit, QAL) is on the library's rig but its free tier holds no skeleton. With them: the mage refitted with `person_rig.py`, and a mesh-stretch check for what the checks missed on it.
- (d) The paid tiers, an owner money decision: Universal Animation Library Pro $9.99 or Source $14.99, Universal Animation Library 2 Source $14.99 (the Standard tiers have 45 and 42 clips), Universal Base Characters Source $19.99 (regular and teen proportions).
**Retry when:** the owner rejects the look at the trial-3 sheet, or a character cannot be had as a model on a skeleton the tool maps (then the cut-out fallback for that one). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-26 ✅ CI plays the browser tests in parallel jobs, and a flake retries one script
The e2e ran as one step, script after script, inside the required job: about 19 of the run's 21 minutes (run 40279020921), and a failure anywhere replayed the whole suite, so a late flake (#337 in `load.ts`, #348 in `shop-stock.ts`) hit the 25-minute timeout and showed CANCELLED. Now `game/e2e/run.ts` holds the groups (the phone opening alone, the other opening runs, three groups of the rest at 140-170 s each) and each group's timeout; CI reads them with `node game/e2e/run.ts --list` and plays each in its own job beside the checks, and "Check, test, build and play" is a gate over all of them. A script that fails is played once more alone; its first attempt's screenshots are kept per group. `pnpm e2e` still plays everything in one go locally; `pnpm e2e:group <group>` plays one.
**Retry when:** the phone opening grows past about 10 minutes (then it is the run's length; split it by `E2E_UNTIL` checkpoints), or the repository turns private and parallel minutes cost money. Model: claude-opus-5-5. Branch `ci/e2e-parallel`.

## 2026-10-26 ✅ The moat: the shared world, the pace, the rights
An imitator can copy the code and the content, both are free by licence. Three things it cannot copy, each now something the repository enforces and not a note. **The shared world:** the creators' extensions, their signatures, the boards and the creator pages live in one valley that grows by pull request; a copy starts empty. **The pace:** the private production line (the owner's sessions, throttles and agent configuration) moves faster than a fork can follow, and it stays out of the public tree: `pnpm private` (tools/src/private-strings.ts, part of `pnpm verify` and so of CI) fails when a tracked file names a home-directory path, a private tool or a personal address, with only the lockfiles allowed. **The rights:** `CLA.md` (a contributor keeps the copyright and grants the project the right to relicense; signed once by a comment, recorded on the branch `cla-signatures` by `.github/workflows/cla.yml`) and `TRADEMARKS.md` (the name and logo are not licensed by the AGPL or CC BY-SA; "made for" is allowed, a fork or another game under the name is not).
Two rules follow. When two features compete for a builder, the one that feeds the shared world or the creator economy (accounts, verified boards, creator pages and points, the market) ranks above the one that does not. And production tooling stays outside the public repo while game tooling stays in it: what a creator or the game's own build needs (`pnpm check`, the bundler, the importer, kit generation, the review skills the pull request template names) is public; the machinery that runs the owner's sessions is not.
The CLA action is CLA Assistant Lite, pinned at `ca4a40a7d1004f18d9960b404b97e5f30a505a08` (v2.6.1, 2024-09-26) after a read of its source: its `dist/` rebuilds byte for byte from `src/` (checked here with `npm ci` and `ncc build`), it calls only the GitHub API with the run's own token, it spawns no process, it ignores commit trailers and reads each commit's GitHub login (all of the owner's recent agent commits resolve to the login `Bobikobi`; a commit whose address is not linked to an account would be named by its git name and would be asked to sign). Findings: the upstream repository is archived (README note, last push 2026-03-23) and declares the node20 runtime, which GitHub is retiring; `npm audit` on its bundled dependencies lists 20 advisories, none on a path that takes anything but GitHub API replies and comment text (a very long comment can stall one run, which the 5-minute timeout ends). It is untested here until the workflow is on `develop`: `pull_request_target` reads the file from the pull request's base branch and `issue_comment` from the default branch, so signing by comment starts working when it reaches `main`. The check is not a required status check (a repository setting, the owner's).
**Needs a lawyer later:** the final CLA wording (above all section 4, which lets the project relicense under any licence while published versions stay available) and the trademark filing, after the name check. **Retry when:** the action stops running on GitHub's runners (then fork it under the owner's account, pinned the same way), or a maintained successor exists. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5. Branch `docs/newcomer-ready`.

## 2026-10-26 🔁 Camera rotation and a deeper HD-2D look are aims for the end of development; until then the pixel game shows the player through what covers it
The owner, 26.10, after a clip of a tilted pixel town where a roof hides the walker: the camera could turn, as a player setting, so that the player sees around what covers them. The owner deferred it. In a pixel game every building, tree and prop would need drawing from four sides, which is four times the art. Every creator's extension would also owe four views, which taxes the shared world the moat rests on. Pixel games of this kind (Stardew Valley, Eastward, Sea of Stars) do not turn the camera. They fade what is in front, outline the hidden body, and plan the map around it. A 3D world turns its camera for nothing.
The same evening the owner closed the 3D question for now (26.10): an HD-2D look (a 3D world with pixel characters, Octopath Traveler's tilt-shift, perspective and light) is examined at the end of development, together with the camera's turn. The 3D door stays paused. Cues that sit on top of the pixel game without changing it (an edge blur, a slight perspective, parallax by a prop's declared height) are built at their own stage in the plan, not now. A started stills trial of the edge blur is kept on the branch `trial/hd2d-look` (c6d04dbd: a world-camera shader behind `?look=`, built but never seen running, no stills yet) for that stage.
Until then: the see-through rule of STYLE.md section 1b (written 18.10, not yet built in `game/src`) is the pixel game's answer. A canopy, roof or tall prop over the player fades the part in front, or shows the player's outline. It is built with the tall props' growth to the table (NEXT `tall-props-to-table`), because those props are what will cover the player. The 50 degree camera already helps: a thing hides ground behind it to about 0.84 of its height (h / tan 50°), against about 1.43 at 35 degrees.
To keep the door open at no cost now: where a prop is made from a 3D source (the anim tool renders the characters from 3D in eight directions), the 3D source is kept with its provenance (docs/art/REUSE.md). A turn of the camera is then a re-render, not a redraw.
**Retry when:** the 3D door resumes (there the turn comes nearly free, in quarter turns, as a setting), or the pixel game's props are mostly rendered from kept 3D sources, so that four views cost a render. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ The luck is keyed: a 128-bit secret and a keyed draw
Finding 3 of the 27.10 security review: `draw(seed, entity, counter)` was a fast 32-bit hash of a 32-bit seed, and a player sees drawn outcomes (the wake pocket, a gather, a search row, a craft, the weather, the `appears` list in every reply). A 2^32 search over a few of them gave the seed, and with it every future draw. Widening the seed alone would not do: a fast unkeyed hash or a linear generator gives its state back from a few outputs.
**Chosen:** the seed is a key of four unsigned 32-bit words (`Seed`, 128 bits), and a draw is the low word of SipHash-2-4 (Aumasson and Bernstein, 2012) of the counter (4 bytes) and the entity (its UTF-16 code units, 2 bytes each) under it, built from 32-bit integer operations in `kernel/src/rng.ts`. Rule R4 holds: a draw is a pure function of key, entity and counter. SipHash over ChaCha8 because it is a keyed function of a message of any length, and an entity is a string; ChaCha would need the entity squeezed into a 96-bit nonce by an unkeyed hash first. Checked against the paper's Appendix A vector and all 64 of the reference `vectors.h`. A draw costs about 2.5 µs on the laptop.
**Old saves:** kernel 0.20, state format 3. `migrateState` widens a 32-bit seed to `[seed, 0, 0, 0]`, the same key wherever it loads, and migration `0007_keyed_luck.sql` does the same to the server's column (now 16 bytes). An old save keeps playing, but its luck differs from before, and a widened key still holds only the 32 bits the old seed had. An opening kept on a phone before the change is let go (no replay can land on it), so that sign-up starts fresh. An account made before it would no longer replay to its stored state, so its board proof fails; the box does not run `AUTH_MODE=session` (the review of 27.10), so no real account is expected to hold such books.
**The phone's own key:** the opening runs under 128 random bits from `crypto.getRandomValues`, which the player holds. It protects nothing from the player during the opening; the caps (one hour, 150 gold) and the replay bound what a chosen key can win. It tells nothing about the account's key, which the server draws from `crypto.randomBytes(16)` at the sign-up and never sends.
**Retry when:** a draw shows in a profile (cache the hash state after the key, or drop to SipHash-1-3), or a world needs keys that rotate. Model: claude-opus-5-5. Branch `fix/secret-luck`.
## 2026-10-27 ✅ The email sign-in finishes in a real browser: the token in the fragment, the link page's own origin, and limits by the proxy's address
The 27.10 security review found that the email link could not complete from a browser: the link page was served with `no-referrer`, under which a form POST carries `Origin: null`, and its CSP `form-action 'self'` blocked the 303 to the game in Chromium and WebKit. The suite was green only because its test browser set Origin by hand. Chosen: the page is `same-origin` and its CSP names the game's origin, the confirm accepts the API's own origin and no other route does, and the token rides in the link's fragment, read by one hashed inline script, so no log holds it. The old `?token=` form is moved to the fragment for one release, then removed. The test browser now opens the link and presses the button the way a browser does, deriving the Origin from the page's referrer policy and checking `form-action` on the redirect. A headless Chrome run on the local pair landed on `#signin=ok` signed in, with no CSP violation. Also: `MAILER=log` starts only beside `ALLOW_LOG_MAILER=staging-only`, the in-process limits count by `CLIENT_ADDRESS_HEADER` (required off loopback), a sign-up checks the name before it replays an opening instead of caching the replay, and the OAuth flow is still spent before the code exchange (the code is single-use at Google, so a reload could not finish anyway).
**Retry when:** the game and the API become one origin (finding 4): the confirm's origin set and `form-action` then collapse to `'self'`. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ A picture drawn from the prompt takes the asked proportion, and the importer drops a glow on the background
The 27.10 world proof drew six large props with qwen from the prompt alone and found two defects in the pipeline. The canvas followed the old 32x32 sprite, not the size asked: a 192x320 cottage was drawn on 1024x1024 and a 64x160 stone came back 60x99 art pixels. And a soft wedge of warm light the model painted on the magenta survived the importer, because the key keeps only the background's own colour: the cottage carried 2868 extra pixels of it (a market stall carried one too), which also widened the box the sprite is fitted to.
**Chosen:** `kaggleCanvasFor` is the one rule for a request's canvas: from the prompt alone (a long-prompt recipe, or denoise 1) the canvas takes the spec's asked width and height, short side `KAGGLE_SIZE`, long side in proportion, multiples of 64 and at most twice the short side; image to image keeps the reference's shape as before. `KAGGLE_NEGATIVE` gains `glow, vignette, light rays, halo`. The importer's `clearGlows` floods from the cleared background through non-outline pixels that are a smooth step apart and clears a region only when it is small, a block rather than a halo ring, of a narrow colour range and open to the background along 0.3 or more of its rim; each bound is measured (DRAWERS.md). A hue band around the background's hue was tried and dropped: it changed all 50 of the first replayed imports.
**Proof:** the same two entries redrawn on the same seed (one kernel run, 617.8 s on a T4): the cottage on 1024x1728, the stone on 1024x2048; the stone came back 60x108 (was 60x99); the cottage still came back with a halo above the roof, and the new key dropped 2041 pixels of it from the import. Replayed on 45 imports of 39 earlier raws, 41 stayed pixel-identical and only the two glow pictures changed. A tall canvas gives a tall subject room, it does not make the model draw one: the cottage is still as wide as it is tall (180x188 for 192x320).
**Retry when:** a glow survives the key (a halo that touches the outline with no dark pixel between), or the kernel's own post-process should drop the glow before the sprite is fitted to the grid, so that the sprite is not drawn smaller around it. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5.

## 2026-10-23 ✅ The tree is data: a graph of any size, laid out from the content

The skill tree is content, not layout code, and its rules hold for hundreds of nodes and many branches. A node has an id, a size (`small`, `major`, `keystone`, or a race's `corner`), an optional price inside its size's band (`params.growth.cost_band`), optional attribute keys, an effect, an optional `cluster` of its school, an optional place (`pos`), and `needs`, which may name a node of another school by its full key. A school is a branch with an optional colour family (`tint`) and direction (`angle`) and named `clusters`. `pnpm check` holds the graph: every need exists, no chain of needs goes round (across schools too), each school hangs together, a price stays in its band, a cluster exists and holds a node, a school places every node or none and no two share a place, and a player of a race sees at most `params.growth.nodes_max` nodes in a school. Where there is no `pos`, `game/src/treelayout.ts` places the nodes from the graph, the same every time.
A generated tree of about three hundred nodes (`tools/test/helpers/bigtree.ts`) proves the rules, the layout and the screen's speed; it lives in the tests only, and the shipped tree stays the five core schools.
Why: the owner, 23.10: the tree must grow far bigger, like the big games compared (Path of Exile, Grim Dawn, the sphere grid of FFX), and a later session designs the full tree; this lays its foundations.
Cost: the format grows by optional fields only, so the saves and the content of the earlier format load unchanged; the seed nodes are still placeholders.
**Retry when:** the session that designs the full tree needs a rule this model cannot say (a node shared by two schools, a choice of one of two), or a tree over the bound draws too slowly. Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ Every draw is kept: an append-only drafts index in the game repo and a private drafts repo for the pictures
Until now a draw that was not picked stayed on one laptop's disk or was lost, and the wrong-draws folder held seven of them by hand. Chosen: `tools/kit-gen/drafts.jsonl` is an append-only index, one record per draft, its id the first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the raw PNG, so identical bytes are one draft that gathers its sources. Review state, notes and later facts are added as `amend` lines, never by editing a line, and `pnpm art:drafts verify --base <ref>` refuses a file that is not the base plus new lines at the end. `kit:gen` adds a line for every draft it imports. The pictures themselves (the raw draw, the kernel's picture before the grid, and what the importer made) live as release assets of the private repo `Bobikobi/GAME-art-drafts`: one release per kernel run, tagged `run-<YYYYMMDD>-<6 hex of the run id>`, nothing ever replaced or deleted. `pnpm art:drafts push|fetch|verify` moves them; verify re-hashes every file against the index and exits 1 on a difference or a missing file. Each record repeats its run's GPU seconds, so the meter sums by run id. The backfill gave the drafts already on disk their records: 965 drafts, 0.58 GB, under `run-backfill-<source>` tags, all `unreviewed` except the seven wrong-draws (`archive`); `docs/art/wrong-draws/library.jsonl` is now a view of the index entries that carry `looks_like`. Not chosen: git LFS or committing the pictures (the repo would grow by the whole archive), and a database (a file in the repo is what an agent can read and diff).
**Retry when:** the drafts repo nears its size limit or a fetch of the whole archive becomes slow (split by quarter, keep the index); or when the picks manifest should read the index instead of its own copies of provenance. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5. Branch `feat/art-drafts`.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ Docs-only pull requests skip the browser tests; the test run's setting stays
A pull request that changes only prose spent the same ~9 minutes in Chrome as one that changes the game (the phone opening alone is about 520 s). Now the job "The browser test groups" (always runs) lists the pull request's changed files with `tools/src/docs-only.ts` (plain node, unit-tested): a pull request is docs only when it changes at least one file and every file is under `docs/`, a Markdown file outside `game/`, `DECISIONS.md`, a file under `.github/prompts/`, or a `tools/kit-gen/*.json` spec. Renames are listed by both paths, an empty or unreadable diff counts as code. The `Play in Chrome` jobs then carry `if: docs_only != 'true'` and are skipped; the checks job (schemas, content, lint, types, unit tests, build, database) always runs. The required check "Check, test, build and play" (the only name in the repository ruleset) is untouched and still runs on every pull request: its gate now accepts a skipped browser job only when `docs_only` is true, and then requires the checks and the group list to have passed and the browser job to be exactly skipped; on a code pull request a skipped job still fails it. A path filter on the workflow was refused again (PLATFORM 9.2: the required check would stay "Expected").
**The test run's setting: none kept.** Measured on the laptop under its own load (about 20), `vitest run --maxWorkers=4`, nice 15, the full suite (160 files, 1867 tests): default 89, 78, 95 and 83 s; `--pool=threads` 125 s (slower); `--no-isolate` 49 and 47 s, and green at 4 workers and at the default, but red at 2 workers (`game/test/session.test.ts`, 2 tests) and under shuffled order (`tools/test/assemble-steps.test.ts`, `server/test/signin.test.ts`): files share module state (the loaded bundle, a mock) and leak into each other when a worker is reused. A flaky unit run is worse than 30 s, so it is not on. `isolate: false` for `kernel/` and `schema/` alone (no mocks): 78 and 68 s against 95 and 83 s in the same minutes, about 15 s, under the 30 s bar. The whole of `pnpm verify` takes 71 s on a CI runner (run 40500064841), so no test-run setting can save a minute there; the browser jobs were the cost. `vitest.config.ts` is unchanged.
**Retry when:** the leaks are fixed (a test that loads the bundle copies it, mocks restore themselves) and a run under `--sequence.shuffle` is green five times, then `isolate: false` is worth about 35 s locally; or the unit run grows past about 3 minutes. The first docs-only pull request after this merges is the live proof: its run must show the five `Play in Chrome` jobs skipped and "Check, test, build and play" green. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5. ## 2026-10-27 ✅ The creators board is verified against the merged history

**CI refuses a pull request that changes `content/board.json` to anything but the board `develop` gets when it lands as one commit; a pull request that leaves the file alone passes.** `pnpm board --base <ref>` computes the board from the base's history plus the branch's own changes as a single commit (the squash), and `--verify` compares the committed file byte for byte; CI runs it on a pull request into `develop` only when the file differs from the base. Before this nothing checked the file: a pull request could write any number into it and the site would show it. The first version verified every pull request, so every branch had to regenerate the board each time `develop` moved; the lander's branch updates do not regenerate, so its first CI run after two other merges went red (run 40656271156). Now the board, like `CREDITS.md`, is regenerated in the release's prep pull request (WORKFLOW.md, Releasing), and between releases `develop`'s board trails its history by the merges since; the site shows production's board, which the release made current.
Raised to the owner on 27.10; his merge of the pull request is the decision.
**Retry when:** the owner lands pull requests by merge commit or rebase instead of squash (the count is then off by the branch's commits), or lets the Actions app bypass the ruleset (a bot could then write the board on `develop` after each merge). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ One name: the account's name is its character's name, in the world

Finding 11 of the 27.10 security review: `accounts.name` was unique, but `state.player.name` came from the request body through the kernel's charset rule only, so two visible "Tess" were possible. **Chosen:** the account name and the character's name are one name, and the server holds the account's side of it, never the kernel. Over the wire (the session mode, where players are accounts), `/action` refuses a `carve` whose name, trimmed, is not the account's, and a `start` whose name is not empty and not the account's; the refusal is a 409 `refused` ("Your name in this world is Wren."), before any step plays, so the state row, its version and its books stay as they were. A `start` with an empty name stays allowed, because the kernel lets the player cut the name later at the bridge's stone; that carve is the one held to the account's name. The names compare as stored, trimmed and exact (the unique index is on the lower-cased name, so "tess" and "Tess" cannot be two accounts, and a character may not show one while the account holds the other). At the sign-up, an opening whose replay carved a name other than the account's is not kept: the account starts fresh, as for any opening that does not prove, and the reply says `fresh`. An opening that carved none yet is kept, and its stone line is held to the account's name over the wire.
**Why not write the name into the replayed state:** the account's log replays the opening step by step (`replayAccount`) and must land on the stored state byte for byte; a rewritten name would be a state no log produces. **Why not refuse the sign-up:** a carved name that another account holds could then never sign up with its opening, and the handover's own rule is that an opening that cannot be proven loses the opening, never the account. The sign-up box already starts with the name the phone carved (`game/src/ui/signup.ts`), so a kept opening is the usual case. The header mode (staging) has no accounts and no tie. Tests: `server/test/signin.test.ts`, `server/test/handover.test.ts`.
**Open:** the game's carve box offers a default name and a free field; for a signed-in account that starts fresh, the stone refuses any name but the account's, so the client should cut the account's name there. The client does not read the account yet (no `/auth/me` in the game), so it waits on the wave that signs the game in.
**Retry when:** accounts may change their name, or two characters may share one account. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5.
## 2026-10-27 ✅ The sign-in e2e drives the real handler: findings 1, 2 and 4 are proven in a browser

**The sign-in e2e plays the real `createSignIn`, not a copy of it.** `game/e2e/signin.ts` runs against `game/test/signin-box.ts`: the real handler with the sign-in tests' `MemoryAuthStore`, a mail outbox the script reads the link from, the real Google provider against `FakeGoogle` (only Google's consent screen is the box's), a fixed clock, and the game's build with an empty `VITE_GAME_SERVER` on the same origin, the one-origin deployment. Headless Chrome chooses every Origin and carries every cookie: the link page's own POST is accepted (finding 1), its 303 reaches `#signin=signup` under the page's CSP `form-action` (finding 2), the game's `GET /state` and `POST /action` resolve to the account by the `__Host-` session cookie (finding 4, on one origin), and the same token posted from a page on another origin is refused with the link unspent. `game/test/fake-auth.ts`, the re-implementation, is gone; its tests run through the box.
The 27.10 security review, the paragraph after its findings table.
**Retry when:** the game and the API move to two origins, or the sign-in's routes, link page or cookies change. Model: claude-opus-5-5.
## 2026-10-27 ✅ One origin: the game is served beside its API

**The game's build and the API share one name on the box, so `APP_ORIGIN` is `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`.** Finding 4 of the 27.10 review: the game at the site's `/play/` and the API on its own name are two origins, the API sends no CORS headers, the client fetches with `credentials: 'same-origin'`, and the `__Host-` session cookie is `SameSite=Lax`, so no signed-in request could carry it. Three ways out: CORS with credentials needs `SameSite=None`, which Safari and the tracking-protection browsers drop, and leaves a second origin to defend; a rewrite through the site's host hides the player's address and country behind the site's edge, so nginx's `limit_req`, the API's limits by `X-Real-IP` and the age gate by country would all count the edge; one origin costs nothing. So `deploy/nginx-openfold.conf` serves `game/dist` as files at the root of the name and proxies the API's five paths, each exact, to the container; the client already names its own origin when `VITE_GAME_SERVER` is set and empty. The boot refuses an `APP_ORIGIN` that differs from `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` unless both are loopback (the laptop's dev client beside its API); unset, it is the public origin. The site's `/play/` stays the demo without a server; pointing the site's door at the name is the cutover (NEXT 18 h6). RUNBOOK step 3 gained the web root and the build's copy, step 5 the restore test, and "What exists" the box's facts of 27.10 (no site on 80 or 443, the auto-updater's swap of the `db` container, no backup cron yet). Tests: `server/test/boot.test.ts` (the guard, the site file's locations).
The owner, 27.10.
**Retry when:** the game is served from a second name (a CDN, a second region): then a shared parent domain or CORS with `SameSite=None`, chosen on purpose. Model: claude-fable-5-1.
## 2026-10-28 ✅ The sign-in link is mailed by Resend

**`MAILER=resend` posts each sign-in link to Resend's API as plain text; `log` stays for a laptop and a staging box.** The owner, 28.10: build the sender, reusing what his other project already runs (Resend, with a send-only key). The mailer is one `fetch` to `https://api.resend.com/emails`, no SDK; a refusal throws with Resend's status and reason and never the key. The boot refuses `MAILER=resend` without the key or without a `MAIL_FROM` that is one address (a name and `<address>`, or a bare address, no line breaks), and its notice never carries the key. Click tracking stays off for the sending domain, since a rewritten link would lose the token after `#`. The sending domain is verified in Resend before the box switches; the game gets its own send-only key, not the other project's. Tests: `server/test/mailer.test.ts`, `server/test/boot.test.ts`.
The owner, 28.10.
**Retry when:** Resend's free allowance is outgrown, or a second sender is needed for another kind of mail (then a mailer per purpose). Model: claude-opus-5-5.

## 2026-10-27 ✅ A vote option is the loop's own comment, under a key the vote recorded when it opened

**`readVote` takes an option only from the vote's own author, under a key the vote's marker lists; any other comment with the option marker in it is a reply.** Before this, every comment on a vote issue carrying `<!-- loop:option:KEY -->` became an option: any GitHub account could add one with a proposal number that was not in the vote, repeat a rival's key to force a tie, or gather its own reactions under a number of its choosing, and the close would label `vote won` / `vote lost` on whatever numbers those comments named, post the loop's "Thank you for this proposal" on them, and publish a merge recommendation the community never voted on (finding F1 of the 27.10 review). Now the marker written at the opening carries the option keys (`options`), `readVote` keeps a comment only when its author is the issue's author and its key is in that list, and a second comment under a key the vote already has is not a second option. A vote opened before the marker held its keys (none is open on 27.10) has the author rule alone. The hub reads votes through the same `readVote`, so the site's vote cards follow. Tests: `tools/test/loop/vote.test.ts` (`optionOf`, the marker's list), `tools/test/loop/orchestration.test.ts` (a stranger's two marker comments, with six reactions on one of them, change neither the count nor the labels at the close).
Proposed by the pull request that builds it; the owner's merge is the decision.
**Retry when:** the loop writes under more than one identity (a maintainer's token beside the Actions token): then the author rule needs the list of identities, and the marker's key list alone carries the vote. Model: claude-fable-5-1.
## 2026-10-27 ✅ The boards accept a kept opening at version 1

**`gapIn` lets the log start at version 1 when that row is a kept opening, and at 2 otherwise.** The sign-up writes a kept opening (NEXT 18 d) in the same transaction as the state row it produced, at the record's version, which is 1 (`createAccount` in `server/src/auth/store-pg.ts`; `signin-db.test.ts` pins the log at `[1]`), while the boards' hole check started every log at 2. So `postScores` threw `ForgedLog` ("the log skips from version 1 to 1") for every account that kept its phone opening, and only accounts that signed up without one could ever be ranked (note of the 27.10 review). The check now takes version 1 as the first row only when that row is `{ kind: 'opening', ... }`, the one row written with the account rather than after it, and steps on from there; any other row at version 1 is refused by name. Everything else stands: a hole is still refused before anything is read, the replay still proves the opening as the first row only, and the ledger still proves its grants. Tests: `server/test/boards.test.ts` (the pure check: an opening at 1 then 2, a skip after it, an ordinary row at 1 refused; against postgres: an account made the way the sign-up makes one, with a kept opening, is scored and stands on the board).
Proposed by the pull request that builds it; the owner's merge is the decision.
**Retry when:** the sign-up writes the kept opening at version 2 (then `FIRST_SEQ` alone is the rule again), or the state row's version stops counting the writes. Model: claude-fable-5-1.

## 2026-10-28 ✅ The art status reads the drafts index: a draft is not archived, and the week is the last seven days

**`art:status` takes `drafted` and the GPU meter from `tools/kit-gen/drafts.jsonl`, not from run folders.** An entry is drafted when the index holds a draft of its name that is not archived, in the entry's own pool when the draft names one (an archived draw is a wrong object kept for another role, so an entry with nothing else stays missing). The meter is each kernel run's own seconds counted once per run id (`gpuSecondsSince`) over the seven days up to now, drafts dated after "now" ignored. On the 27.10 index that reads 198.2 GPU minutes from 32 runs, where adding the seconds draft by draft would say 2332.7. Each pool also prints its drafts by review state, archived ones included. Kaggle documents no weekly reset day (third-party sources say Saturday, Sunday, or a rolling window), so the window rolls: against a fixed reset it can only over-count, which errs on the side of the floor. Tests: `tools/test/art-status.test.ts` (the fixture index, the real index at a fixed date).
Proposed by the follow-up of #374; the owner's merge is the decision.
**Retry when:** Kaggle's quota page or a kernel's own reply names the reset day or the hours left: then the window starts there, and the meter can read the account's own figure. ## 2026-10-28 ✅ The server looks the country up itself: DB-IP's free file in the image, read by a pure-JavaScript reader

**The age limit's country comes from the client's address, looked up in a file inside the API's image, not from a module on the host.** The owner refused a root apt step on the shared box (28.10), so the nginx GeoIP2 route of RUNBOOK step 6 is dropped. The API reads the address nginx names (`X-Real-IP`, which a client cannot set) and looks it up in DB-IP's free country file (CC BY 4.0), fetched in the Dockerfile from a pinned month and checked against its sha256, read with `mmdb-lib` pinned at 3.0.3 (MIT, no dependencies, 10 KB; scanned before use: no network, file, process or eval calls, no install scripts). `COUNTRY_HEADER` stays as an override that wins when it carries a plain two-capital code. A private or unknown address, a string that is not one address, and a code that names no place (`ZZ`, `XX`) are no country and hold the sign-up to the highest age, so a miss never lowers the bar. A file that does not open stops the boot with a line naming `COUNTRY_DB`.

Evidence: unit tests over a fixture built in the test (IPv4, IPv6, an IPv4 address written as IPv6, garbage, `ZZ`), sign-up tests where the address and the header decide (a 15-year-old is in from Israel, out from Germany), boot tests; against the real 8 MB file, the bundled reader answered 8.8.8.8 US, 2.55.0.1 IL, 5.9.0.1 DE, IPv6 and mapped forms right, private and loopback addresses none; the built image booted with the file, refused a file that is not a database, and ran without it when `COUNTRY_DB` is empty. Credit: the game's credits panel and the README. A new month is two changed lines in `deploy/Dockerfile`.

**Retry when:** the file's accuracy for a country the game opens to proves too coarse (then MaxMind's GeoLite2, which needs a licence key), or the box gets a CDN in front, which brings its own country header (then `COUNTRY_HEADER` is the way). Model: claude-sonnet-5-5.

## 2026-10-28 ✅ Height enters the walk: one step rule for `walkDistance`, the server's check and the tools (kernel 0.21.0)

NEXT 19 item c, G68. `makeStepRule(bundle)` in `kernel/src/world.ts` decides a step between two four-adjacent tiles: the same level is open, a change of 1 to 3 levels is open only when one of its two ends is a crossing (a prop with `crossing: true`), and a change of more than 3 is never walked. Either end counts because the schema puts the crossing on the lower tile: walking up, the crossing is the tile left; walking down, the tile entered. A map with no `levels` answers true, so a flat map is untouched.
`walkDistance` (G56's BFS, so `validateTravel` and the server's charge) and the tools' `reachable` (`tools/src/reach.ts`) and `walkFrom` (`tools/src/balance/reckon.ts`) call it; a local session still trusts a longer hop and checks no path, as before. On the valley as it stands no slot is cut off (the three wakes reach every slot) and 8 are reached by a longer way. An old save whose tile the faces wall off from every wake is stood on the nearest reachable tile by `relocationOf` (`kernel/src/relocate.ts`; inside the same slot when it has one, ties to the smaller row then column), called from `adapt()` in `game/src/session.ts`: the tile changes, the bag, health and clock do not. `KERNEL_VERSION` 0.20.0 to 0.21.0.
The client's feet read the same rule (items d and e), so the server has nothing to refuse that the phone allowed. `canEnter` (`game/src/walking.ts`) takes the tile the body leaves, the player's own when none is given, and asks `stepRuleFor(bundle)`, the function `walkDistance` reads, built once per bundle. The `Walker` asks it on the edge it crosses: a line of tiles is entered from the one behind it, and the tiles of the line lie side by side across the box, each from the one before, so a refused edge is never crossed and never lain across. A body astride a crossing that presses down along a face is eased into the lane of the tile its feet stand in, by the corner ease the walk already had. The searches that pick a route ask the rule per step: the peek's line (`game/src/peek.ts`), the wolf's run (`game/src/predator.ts`) and the planners of the e2e, which hand the tile they expand as `from`. Tests: `game/test/height-walk.test.ts` (the face refused and the crossing open both ways, the mountain band, 150 seeded walks whose every told tile the kernel takes with `validateTravel`, the three searches over the crossing on a small map, the valley's town face at x=21|22 and its steps at (28,14)).
**Open:** the level-blind searches in `tools/src/hazards.ts`, `starts.ts`, `mapplan.ts` and `play.ts` wait for item g. There is no tap-to-walk in `game/src` yet: when it is built, its path search asks the same rule (`canEnter` with `from`). Model: claude-sonnet-5-5.

Found with the walk fixed: the collapsed hall's road by day (the lane south of the ruins, door at 37,32) runs down x=45 past the lone log at 45,25, which blocks its tile while ready, and the old route over the hill and the gap at 39,29 steps from level 1 to level 0 with no crossing, which the rule refuses. The script now takes a ready node that shuts a road (`__e2e.blocker`, `Player.clear`) as a player does; the walk from Pell's door to a dig spot is 43-47 steps, against 25-29 before. Found next, in a local play of `opening-phone`: after six digs (six pottery shards, no whole bowl) the loop came back to `core/ruins-hall#0` standing on 38,29, the stand of the sixth, and the planner counted its own tile as a goal facing left; the step behind it, 39,29, is that gap, so `FreeWalk.turnTo` could neither turn in place (a dig spot ahead is an open tile) nor step back and return: `cannot turn toward core/ruins-hall#0 at 38,29: both tiles beside it are closed`. Before the rule reached the client that step back was open. The in-page search now ends where the last turn can be made (the tile ahead closed, or the tile behind open) and falls back to any goal only when no walk reaches such a tile; the test plays the dig loop from every stand to every spot, the same spot again too (red without the filter: `38,29 > 37,29: the stand 38,29 cannot face left`).

## 2026-10-29 ✅ Chunk streaming: the 3x3 ring, dropped beyond one ring, baked in idle slices

NEXT 19 item d. `game/src/stream.ts`: `ChunkStream` holds the 3 by 3 world chunks (64 tiles a side) around the player's, loads on a crossing and drops what is beyond one ring first, so the count never passes 9 (a counter, `peak`, asserted on a walk across a 3 by 3 test world and on an 8-chunk walk). `IdleQueue` bakes the 16-tile pages the ring needs one page a slice (`requestIdleCallback`, a timer where there is none; a slice shorter than the last page waits, up to 120 ms, then runs one); the pages the view touches still bake at once. The scene feeds the stream the player's tile and `GroundChunks` bakes only pages of held chunks; the valley is one chunk, so it plays as before.
Measured in headless Chromium at 480x1040, device scale 3, walking 4.5 tiles a second over two chunk and page borders in a cloud sandbox (software canvas): the longest frame 46-73 ms before, 31-32 ms after; the p95 17.0-17.9 before and 17.1-19.2 after (not a gain, the frames were already at the 60 Hz tick); one page's bake 8-11 ms in the browser, 1.1 ms median for the blit alone (vitest); 12 page textures, 50 MB, at any moment, before and after (the whole 3x3 world at density 4 would be about 600 MB). The seam: the window's edge is at least 64 tiles from the player and the mist is solid 11 tiles out, so a page not held is never on screen (asserted).
**Retry when:** a real phone shows a gap at a border (then the ring grows to the pages the camera sees, not the chunks), or the world has more than one chunk file and the loader must be async (then `load` returns a promise and `update` keeps the old window until it lands). The 3D door reads the same chunk files and builds nothing here. Model: claude-sonnet-5-5.

## 2026-10-29 ✅ The gaits: walk, run on hold, sneak as a toggle; the wolf hears by the gait

The kernel's `move` action carries `gait` (`walk` default, so an old save, an old log and an old client read walk): a run takes `walk_step_ms` divided by `gait.run_div` (190 ms to 95), a sneak times `gait.sneak_mul` (to 380), exact integers from `gaitStepMs`, and the reach a move may cover in the time that passed follows the gait's step. The wolf's cone reads the gait: its length is the wolf's eyes (5) moved by the noise table (still 2, sneak 3, walk 5, run 8), so a run is seen at 8 tiles, a walk at 5, a sneak at 3, cover still hides and an adjacent tile is always seen (`hearingOf`, called by `sees`). `KERNEL_VERSION` 0.21.0 to 0.21.1.
The client: the thumb held on the stick past 400 ms runs (one thumb on a phone, no double tap; Shift on a keyboard), and a "Sneak" toggle beside A and B in frame D (16 px, 44 px at the least) sneaks; the body's tween speed is the gait's step time.
**Breath:** no bar exists in the player's state (breath is only a refill rate in a fight), so a run costs nothing yet and `gait.run_breath_cost` waits for the fight kernel. **Open:** the server's move report carries no gait, so online a run is still checked at the walk's pace; the still radius (2) is in the table but a standing player is still watched at the walk's cone; the hop is the owner's.
**Retry when:** the fight kernel brings the breath bar (price the run then), or a phone loop shows the 400 ms hold firing by accident.
